```cpp
#pragma once

#include <cstdint>
#include <memory>
#include <optional>
#include <span>
#include <string>
#include <string_view>
#include <vector>

#include "dvv/base/error.h"
#include "dvv/base/lifetime.h"
#include "dvv/gpu/device_id.h"
#include "dvv/gpu/gpu_info.h"

namespace dvv::gpu {

namespace detail {
class InstanceCore;
}  // namespace detail

// Vulkan validation (VK_LAYER_KHRONOS_validation). Sync adds synchronization validation.
enum class ValidationMode : std::uint8_t { Off, On, Sync };

[[nodiscard]] std::string_view validationModeName(ValidationMode mode) noexcept;

// "major.minor.patch" of a Vulkan version number (VK_MAKE_API_VERSION encoding; the variant bits
// are ignored).
[[nodiscard]] std::string formatVulkanVersion(std::uint32_t version);

struct GpuRegistryOptions {
    std::string applicationName = "dvv";
    // Unset: the environment variable DVV_VK_VALIDATION (off, on or sync); without it On in Debug
    // builds and Off in Release builds. A requested layer that is not installed turns validation
    // off with a log line, unless validation is required.
    std::optional<ValidationMode> validation = std::nullopt;
    // Unset: DVV_VK_VALIDATION_REQUIRED (1 or 0, default 0). When true, a requested validation
    // layer that is not installed is an error (Errc::Unsupported); used by CI.
    std::optional<bool> validationRequired = std::nullopt;
};

// The process's Vulkan instance (spec §5.2): loads the Vulkan loader at run time, creates a
// headless instance (Vulkan 1.4 API on a loader of at least 1.3), routes debug messages to the dvv
// Logger, and records and ranks the physical devices. Its own creation authority; thread-safe.
class GpuRegistry final : public Managed<GpuRegistry>,
                          public std::enable_shared_from_this<GpuRegistry> {
public:
    // Errc::Unsupported "Vulkan loader not found" without a loader; Errc::Unsupported when the
    // loader is older than 1.3 or a required validation layer is missing; Errc::Format for a bad
    // DVV_VK_VALIDATION or DVV_VK_VALIDATION_REQUIRED value.
    [[nodiscard]] static Result<std::shared_ptr<GpuRegistry>> create(
        const GpuRegistryOptions& options = {});

    GpuRegistry(CreationKey<GpuRegistry> key, std::unique_ptr<detail::InstanceCore> instance,
                std::vector<GpuInfo> devices, std::vector<DeviceId> ranked) noexcept;
    ~GpuRegistry();

    // The validation actually enabled (Off when the layer was requested but is not installed).
    [[nodiscard]] ValidationMode validation() const noexcept;
    // Debug messages that count as failures: errors of any type, and validation or performance
    // warnings. Every message is logged; tests require this count to stay zero.
    [[nodiscard]] std::uint64_t validationMessageCount() const noexcept;
    // Version of the Vulkan loader (VK_MAKE_API_VERSION encoding).
    [[nodiscard]] std::uint32_t loaderVersion() const noexcept;

    // Every physical device in enumeration order: devices()[id.index()].
    [[nodiscard]] std::span<const GpuInfo> devices() const noexcept { return devices_; }
    // Every device id, best first (rankDevices()).
    [[nodiscard]] std::span<const DeviceId> rankedDevices() const noexcept { return ranked_; }
    // The best device that meets the requirements; nullopt when none does.
    [[nodiscard]] std::optional<DeviceId> bestDevice() const noexcept;

private:
    std::unique_ptr<detail::InstanceCore> instance_;
    std::vector<GpuInfo> devices_;
    std::vector<DeviceId> ranked_;
};

}  // namespace dvv::gpu
```

`dvv-gpu/src/gpu_registry.cpp` (replace the whole file; new: `create()` enumerates the devices, hands the handles to the `InstanceCore`, ranks the infos and logs them; the constructor; `bestDevice()`):

```cpp
#include "dvv/gpu/gpu_registry.h"

#include <algorithm>
#include <format>
#include <utility>

#include "devices.h"
#include "dvv/base/log.h"
#include "instance.h"

namespace dvv::gpu {

static_assert(ManagedType<GpuRegistry>);

std::string_view validationModeName(ValidationMode mode) noexcept {
    switch (mode) {
        case ValidationMode::Off: return "off";
        case ValidationMode::On: return "on";
        case ValidationMode::Sync: return "sync";
    }
    return "?";
}

std::string formatVulkanVersion(std::uint32_t version) {
    return std::format("{}.{}.{}", VK_API_VERSION_MAJOR(version), VK_API_VERSION_MINOR(version),
                       VK_API_VERSION_PATCH(version));
}

Result<std::shared_ptr<GpuRegistry>> GpuRegistry::create(const GpuRegistryOptions& options) {
    DVV_TRY(auto instance, detail::InstanceCore::create(options));
    DVV_TRY(auto enumeration, detail::enumerateDevices(instance->instance(), instance->dispatch()));
    instance->setPhysicalDevices(std::move(enumeration.devices));
    std::vector<DeviceId> ranked = rankDevices(enumeration.infos);
    for (const GpuInfo& info : enumeration.infos) {
        DVV_LOG_INFO("Vulkan device {}: {} ({}, Vulkan {}){}", info.id, info.name,
                     deviceTypeName(info.type), formatVulkanVersion(info.apiVersion),
                     info.usable() ? "" : ", not usable");
    }
    return std::make_shared<GpuRegistry>(
        CreationKey<GpuRegistry>(LifetimeRegistry::instance().issue<GpuRegistry>()),
        std::move(instance), std::move(enumeration.infos), std::move(ranked));
}

GpuRegistry::GpuRegistry(CreationKey<GpuRegistry> key,
                         std::unique_ptr<detail::InstanceCore> instance,
                         std::vector<GpuInfo> devices, std::vector<DeviceId> ranked) noexcept
    : Managed(std::move(key)),
      instance_(std::move(instance)),
      devices_(std::move(devices)),
      ranked_(std::move(ranked)) {}

GpuRegistry::~GpuRegistry() = default;

ValidationMode GpuRegistry::validation() const noexcept {
    return instance_->validation();
}

std::uint64_t GpuRegistry::validationMessageCount() const noexcept {
    return instance_->validationMessageCount();
}

std::uint32_t GpuRegistry::loaderVersion() const noexcept {
    return instance_->loaderVersion();
}

std::optional<DeviceId> GpuRegistry::bestDevice() const noexcept {
    if (ranked_.empty() || !devices_[ranked_.front().index()].usable()) {
        return std::nullopt;
    }
    return ranked_.front();
}

}  // namespace dvv::gpu
```

`dvv-gpu/tools/dvv_gpu_info.cpp`:

```cpp
// dvv-gpu-info: prints what the GpuRegistry finds (spec §5.1).
//   dvv-gpu-info [--json]
// Exit codes (spec §10.3): 0 a usable device exists, 1 bad arguments, 4 no Vulkan loader or no
// usable device.

#include <cstdio>
#include <exception>
#include <format>
#include <memory>
#include <print>
#include <string>
#include <string_view>

#include "dvv/base/error.h"
#include "dvv/base/json.h"
#include "dvv/base/leak_check.h"
#include "dvv/base/log.h"
#include "dvv/gpu/gpu_info.h"
#include "dvv/gpu/gpu_registry.h"

namespace {

constexpr int EXIT_OK = 0;
constexpr int EXIT_BAD_ARGUMENTS = 1;
constexpr int EXIT_GPU_ERROR = 4;

std::string yesNo(bool value) {
    return value ? "yes" : "no";
}

std::string formatBytes(std::uint64_t bytes) {
    constexpr double GIB = 1024.0 * 1024.0 * 1024.0;
    return std::format("{:.1f} GiB", static_cast<double>(bytes) / GIB);
}

void printText(const dvv::gpu::GpuRegistry& registry) {
    const auto best = registry.bestDevice();
    std::println("Vulkan loader {}, validation {}, {} device(s)",
                 dvv::gpu::formatVulkanVersion(registry.loaderVersion()),
                 dvv::gpu::validationModeName(registry.validation()), registry.devices().size());
    for (const dvv::gpu::DeviceId id : registry.rankedDevices()) {
        const dvv::gpu::GpuInfo& info = registry.devices()[id.index()];
        std::println("");
        std::println("Device {}: {} ({}){}", id, info.name, dvv::gpu::deviceTypeName(info.type),
                     best == id ? ", selected" : "");
        std::println("  Vulkan {}, vendor {:#06x}, device {:#06x}",
                     dvv::gpu::formatVulkanVersion(info.apiVersion), info.vendorId, info.productId);
        std::println("  driver {} {} (version {:#x})", info.driverName, info.driverInfo,
                     info.driverVersion);
        std::println("  device UUID {}", dvv::gpu::formatUuid(info.deviceUuid));
        std::println("  device-local memory {}", formatBytes(info.deviceLocalMemoryBytes));
        std::println(
            "  optional: shaderInt16 {}, shaderFloat16 {}, hostImageCopy {}, "
            "VK_EXT_memory_budget {}",
            yesNo(info.optional.shaderInt16), yesNo(info.optional.shaderFloat16),
            yesNo(info.optional.hostImageCopy), yesNo(info.optional.memoryBudget));
        if (info.usable()) {
            std::println("  usable");
        } else {
            std::string missing;
            for (const std::string& requirement : info.missingRequirements) {
                missing += missing.empty() ? requirement : ", " + requirement;
            }
            std::println("  not usable, missing: {}", missing);
        }
    }
}

void printJson(const dvv::gpu::GpuRegistry& registry) {
    dvv::json::Value devices = dvv::json::Value::array();
    for (const dvv::gpu::DeviceId id : registry.rankedDevices()) {
        devices.push_back(dvv::gpu::toJson(registry.devices()[id.index()]));
    }
    const auto best = registry.bestDevice();
    const dvv::json::Value output{
        {"loaderVersion", dvv::gpu::formatVulkanVersion(registry.loaderVersion())},
        {"validation", dvv::gpu::validationModeName(registry.validation())},
        {"selectedDevice", best ? dvv::json::Value(best->index()) : dvv::json::Value(nullptr)},
        {"devices", std::move(devices)},
    };
    std::println("{}", dvv::json::dump(output, 2));
}

void printError(const dvv::Error& error, bool json) {
    if (json) {
        const dvv::json::Value output{
            {"error", {{"code", dvv::errcName(error.code)}, {"message", error.describe()}}}};
        std::println("{}", dvv::json::dump(output, 2));
    } else {
        std::println(stderr, "dvv-gpu-info: {}", error.describe());
    }
}

int run(int argc, char** argv) {
    bool json = false;
    for (int i = 1; i < argc; ++i) {
        const std::string_view argument(argv[i]);
        if (argument == "--json") {
            json = true;
        } else if (argument == "--help" || argument == "-h") {
            std::println("usage: dvv-gpu-info [--json]");
            return EXIT_OK;
        } else {
            std::println(stderr,
                         "dvv-gpu-info: unknown argument '{}'\nusage: dvv-gpu-info [--json]",
                         argument);
            return EXIT_BAD_ARGUMENTS;
        }
    }

    const auto registry = dvv::gpu::GpuRegistry::create();
    if (!registry) {
        printError(registry.error(), json);
        return EXIT_GPU_ERROR;
    }
    if (json) {
        printJson(**registry);
    } else {
        printText(**registry);
    }
    return (*registry)->bestDevice() ? EXIT_OK : EXIT_GPU_ERROR;
}

}  // namespace

int main(int argc, char** argv) {
    try {
        dvv::Logger::instance().addSink(std::make_shared<dvv::StderrSink>());
        dvv::Logger::instance().setLevel(dvv::LogLevel::Warn);
        const int exit_code = run(argc, argv);
        return dvv::finishWithLeakCheck(exit_code);
    } catch (const std::exception& error) {
        std::fprintf(stderr, "dvv-gpu-info: %s\n", error.what());
        return EXIT_GPU_ERROR;
    }
}
```

```cmake
dvv_set_compiler_options(dvv_gpu)

install(TARGETS dvv_gpu EXPORT dvv_gpuTargets)
```

with

```cmake
dvv_set_compiler_options(dvv_gpu)

# dvv-gpu-info: prints every Vulkan device the registry finds (text or --json).
add_executable(dvv_gpu_info tools/dvv_gpu_info.cpp)
set_target_properties(dvv_gpu_info PROPERTIES OUTPUT_NAME dvv-gpu-info)
target_link_libraries(dvv_gpu_info PRIVATE dvv::gpu)
dvv_set_compiler_options(dvv_gpu_info)

install(TARGETS dvv_gpu EXPORT dvv_gpuTargets)
install(TARGETS dvv_gpu_info)
```

Build and run it on Windows with the prefix's validation layer and synchronization validation (run the whole block in one tool call; the tool does not keep environment variables between calls):

```powershell
Push-Location dvv-gpu; cmake --workflow --preset vs2026; Pop-Location
$env:VK_ADD_LAYER_PATH = (Resolve-Path _third_party/msvc/bin).Path; $env:DVV_VK_VALIDATION = 'sync'
& _build/dvv-gpu/vs2026/Debug/dvv-gpu-info.exe; "exit $LASTEXITCODE"
& _build/dvv-gpu/vs2026/Debug/dvv-gpu-info.exe --json; "exit $LASTEXITCODE"
& _build/dvv-gpu/vs2026/Debug/dvv-gpu-info.exe --no-such-option; "exit $LASTEXITCODE"
Remove-Item Env:VK_ADD_LAYER_PATH, Env:DVV_VK_VALIDATION
```

Expected: the workflow passes as in Step 4. The text output starts with `Vulkan loader <x.y.z>, validation sync, <n> device(s)`, then one block per device, best first: `Device <id>: <name> (<type>), selected` for the best usable device, then the lines `Vulkan ..., vendor 0x...., device 0x....`, `driver ...`, `device UUID ...`, `device-local memory <x.y> GiB`, `optional: shaderInt16 ..., shaderFloat16 ..., hostImageCopy ..., VK_EXT_memory_budget ...` and `usable` (or `not usable, missing: ...`); `exit 0`. `--json` prints an object with `loaderVersion`, `validation` (`"sync"`), `selectedDevice` and `devices` (the same order, each with the fields of `toJson`); `exit 0`. `--no-such-option` prints `dvv-gpu-info: unknown argument '--no-such-option'` and the usage line to stderr; `exit 1`. No `Vulkan validation warning` or `Vulkan validation error` line appears on stderr. On a machine without a Vulkan loader or without a usable Vulkan 1.4 device the first two runs print the reason and exit with 4.

```powershell
wsl -d Ubuntu-24.04 -e bash -lc '. _build/tools/linux/env.sh && cd dvv-gpu && cmake --workflow --preset ninja-clang 2>&1 | grep -E "tests passed|tests failed|error"; cd .. && VK_ADD_LAYER_PATH=$PWD/_third_party/clang/share/vulkan/explicit_layer.d DVV_VK_VALIDATION=sync DVV_VK_VALIDATION_REQUIRED=1 _build/dvv-gpu/ninja-clang/Debug/dvv-gpu-info --json; echo exit $?'
```

```powershell
GpuRegistry records every physical device as a GpuInfo: id, name, type, vendor and device ids,
Vulkan and driver versions, driver name and info, device UUID, device-local memory, the
requirements of spec §5.1 it misses (Vulkan 1.4, a universal queue family, each required feature)
and the optional features (shaderInt16, shaderFloat16, hostImageCopy, VK_EXT_memory_budget).
Feature structures above a device's Vulkan version are not queried. One list of required features
serves both the check and, later, device creation.

discrete > integrated > virtual > CPU > other, then device-local memory), bestDevice() the first
usable one. GpuInfo converts to JSON.

dvv-gpu-info prints the devices as text or --json and exits 0 when a usable device exists, 4 when
not or without a loader, 1 on bad arguments (spec §10.3).
```

### Task 15: GPU test harness

**Files:**
- Create: `dvv-gpu/src/access.h`, `dvv-gpu/tests/gpu_main.cpp`, `dvv-gpu/tests/gpu_fixture.h`, `dvv-gpu/tests/gpu_fixture.cpp`, `dvv-gpu/tests/gpu_test_registry.cpp`, `dvv-gpu/tests/run-gpu-info.cmake`
- Modify: `dvv-gpu/include/dvv/gpu/gpu_registry.h`, `dvv-gpu/tests/CMakeLists.txt`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Task 13 (`GpuRegistry::create`, `validation()`, `validationMessageCount()`, `loaderVersion()`, `validationModeName`, `ValidationMode`; `detail::InstanceCore::instance()`, `dispatch()`, `debugUtils()`), Task 14 (`devices()`, `rankedDevices()`, `bestDevice()`, `GpuInfo`, `InstanceCore::physicalDevices()`, the `dvv_gpu_info` executable and its exit codes). dvv-base: `dvv::getEnvironment(std::string_view) -> std::optional<std::string>`, `dvv::setEnvironment(std::string_view name, std::string_view value) -> bool` (an empty value removes the variable), `dvv::Result<T>`, `dvv::Error` (`code`, `describe()`), `dvv::Errc::Unsupported`, `dvv::Logger::instance()` with `addSink`, `clearSinks()`, `setLevel`, `dvv::StderrSink`, `dvv::MemorySink` with `std::vector<std::string> lines() const`, `dvv::LifetimeRegistry::instance().setDetailed(bool)`, `dvv::finishWithLeakCheck(int)`, CMake `DVV_TEST_ENVIRONMENT`.
- Produces (C++, private, `src/access.h`): `struct dvv::gpu::detail::Access { static InstanceCore& instance(const GpuRegistry&) noexcept; }`, a friend of `GpuRegistry` (`friend struct detail::Access;`), for dvv-gpu's own sources and white-box tests; later tasks add members for their classes.
- Produces (tests, `tests/gpu_fixture.h`, namespace `dvv::gpu::test`): `class GpuTest` (base of every GPU test through `TEST_CASE_FIXTURE`): its constructor creates a registry from the environment (`DVV_VK_VALIDATION`, `DVV_VK_VALIDATION_REQUIRED`) and fails the test when that fails; `GpuRegistry& registry() const noexcept`; `DeviceId device() const` (the best usable device); the destructor checks `registry().validationMessageCount() == 0`.
- Produces (CMake, `tests/CMakeLists.txt`): GPU test executable `dvv_gpu_device_tests` (sources `gpu_fixture.cpp`, `gpu_main.cpp`, `gpu_test_registry.cpp`; later tasks add their `gpu_test_*.cpp` to this list), CTest tests `dvv_gpu.gpu` (the executable) and `dvv_gpu.gpu_info` (`cmake -DTOOL=<dvv-gpu-info> -P tests/run-gpu-info.cmake`), both with `LABELS gpu`, `SKIP_RETURN_CODE 77`, `TIMEOUT 300`, `ENVIRONMENT DVV_LEAK_CHECK=fail;VK_ADD_LAYER_PATH=<directory of the prefix's VkLayer_khronos_validation.json>` and `ENVIRONMENT_MODIFICATION ${DVV_TEST_ENVIRONMENT}`; cache variable `DVV_VK_VALIDATION_LAYER_MANIFEST` (found under `<prefix>/bin` on Windows, `<prefix>/share/vulkan/explicit_layer.d` on Linux).
- Behaviour: `main()` of the GPU tests probes with validation off; without a loader or a usable Vulkan 1.4 device (`GpuRegistry::create` fails with `Errc::Unsupported`, or `bestDevice()` is empty) it prints `GPU tests skipped: <reason>` and exits with 77 (CTest: skipped), or prints `GPU tests failed: DVV_REQUIRE_GPU=1, but <reason>` and exits with 1 when `DVV_REQUIRE_GPU=1`. Any other registry failure (for example `Errc::Internal` from instance creation) is not a missing GPU: it prints `GPU tests failed: <error>` and exits with 1 in every environment. Otherwise it runs doctest with a stderr log sink at Warn, then the leak check. `run-gpu-info.cmake` exits with 77 the same way when the tool exits with 4.

Note: the GPU test sources are top-level `tests/gpu_*.cpp` (not `tests/gpu/`), so the clang-tidy pathspec `:(glob)tests/*.cpp` covers them. The case "a deliberate validation error is counted" is the positive control the Task 13 review asked for: with validation on or sync it makes a deliberate invalid call (`vkGetPhysicalDeviceImageFormatProperties2` with image usage 0) and requires the message count to rise and a `Vulkan validation error` log line; with validation off it only prints a MESSAGE. (The spike's fill-buffer hazard would need a command buffer, which arrives in a later task; the image-usage check works at registry level.) The last case, "a validation layer that cannot be loaded turns validation off without a count", checks Task 13's layer fallback end to end: it writes a `VkLayer_khronos_validation.json` whose library does not exist into a temporary directory, makes it the only layer the loader sees (`VK_LAYER_PATH` set, `VK_ADD_LAYER_PATH` removed, both restored afterwards), and requires validation `Off`, a message count of zero and the warning `VK_LAYER_KHRONOS_validation could not be loaded; validation is off`; with validation required, `create()` fails with `Unsupported` or `OutOfMemory` (the loader's result, converted). It does not use the fixture, because its registries must not see the real layer. Linux CI does not install Mesa yet, so CI's GPU tests skip (77) until the CI task (Task 23) adds lavapipe and `DVV_REQUIRE_GPU=1`; Windows CI runners have no GPU and keep skipping. Every Windows run below sets `DVV_VK_VALIDATION=sync` in the same command; every Linux run sets `DVV_REQUIRE_GPU=1 DVV_VK_VALIDATION=sync DVV_VK_VALIDATION_REQUIRED=1`, as CI will. Windows workflows can take several minutes: use a 10-minute timeout or run them in the background.

Note (review fixes): `missingGpu()` returns `Result<std::optional<std::string>>` and treats only `Errc::Unsupported` as a missing GPU, so a regression such as an `Errc::Internal` instance failure fails the GPU tests instead of hiding as a skip on machines without `DVV_REQUIRE_GPU=1`; the fake-layer case above was added with the Task 13 count reset.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

`dvv-gpu/tests/gpu_main.cpp`:

```cpp
// GPU tests (CTest label gpu). Without a usable Vulkan 1.4 device the executable exits with 77,
// which CTest reports as skipped (SKIP_RETURN_CODE), unless DVV_REQUIRE_GPU=1 turns that into a
// failure (CI on lavapipe).
#define DOCTEST_CONFIG_IMPLEMENT
#include <doctest/doctest.h>

#include <cstdio>
#include <exception>
#include <expected>
#include <memory>
#include <optional>
#include <print>
#include <string>

#include "dvv/base/environment.h"
#include "dvv/base/error.h"
#include "dvv/base/leak_check.h"
#include "dvv/base/lifetime.h"
#include "dvv/base/log.h"
#include "dvv/gpu/gpu_registry.h"

namespace {

constexpr int EXIT_SKIPPED = 77;

// Why the tests cannot run here: nullopt when a usable device exists, the reason when there is no
// loader or no usable Vulkan 1.4 device (Errc::Unsupported), an error for any other failure.
// Validation is off for this probe; the tests themselves validate.
dvv::Result<std::optional<std::string>> missingGpu() {
    const auto registry =
        dvv::gpu::GpuRegistry::create({.validation = dvv::gpu::ValidationMode::Off});
    if (!registry) {
        if (registry.error().code != dvv::Errc::Unsupported) {
            return std::unexpected(registry.error());
        }
        return registry.error().describe();
    }
    if (!(*registry)->bestDevice()) {
        return "no Vulkan 1.4 device meets the requirements";
    }
    return std::nullopt;
}

int run(int argc, char** argv) {
    dvv::LifetimeRegistry::instance().setDetailed(true);
    // Validation messages and other warnings appear in the test output.
    dvv::Logger::instance().addSink(std::make_shared<dvv::StderrSink>());
    dvv::Logger::instance().setLevel(dvv::LogLevel::Warn);

    const auto probed = missingGpu();
    if (!probed) {
        std::println(stderr, "GPU tests failed: {}", probed.error().describe());
        return 1;
    }
    if (const std::optional<std::string>& missing = *probed; missing.has_value()) {
        if (dvv::getEnvironment("DVV_REQUIRE_GPU") == "1") {
            std::println(stderr, "GPU tests failed: DVV_REQUIRE_GPU=1, but {}", *missing);
            return 1;
        }
        std::println("GPU tests skipped: {}", *missing);
        return EXIT_SKIPPED;
    }

    doctest::Context context(argc, argv);
    const int result = context.run();
    if (context.shouldExit()) {
        return result;
    }
    return dvv::finishWithLeakCheck(result);
}

}  // namespace

int main(int argc, char** argv) {
    try {
        return run(argc, argv);
    } catch (const std::exception& error) {
        std::fprintf(stderr, "GPU tests failed: %s\n", error.what());
        return 1;
    }
}
```

```cpp
#pragma once

namespace dvv::gpu::test {

// Base of every GPU test (TEST_CASE_FIXTURE): a registry configured from the environment

class GpuTest {

    GpuTest(const GpuTest&) = delete;

    GpuTest(GpuTest&&) = delete;
    GpuTest& operator=(GpuTest&&) = delete;

    // The best usable device (main() checked that one exists).

};
```

```cpp
    if (!registry) {
        FAIL(registry.error().describe());
    }
    registry_ = std::move(*registry);
}

GpuTest::~GpuTest() {
    CHECK(registry_->validationMessageCount() == 0);
}
```

`dvv-gpu/tests/gpu_test_registry.cpp` (device enumeration and ranking, validation following `DVV_VK_VALIDATION`, the messenger policy end to end through `vkSubmitDebugUtilsMessageEXT`, the positive control, and the fallback for a validation layer that cannot be loaded):

```cpp
#include <doctest/doctest.h>

#include <algorithm>
#include <atomic>
#include <chrono>
#include <cstdint>
#include <filesystem>
#include <format>
#include <fstream>
#include <memory>
#include <optional>
#include <string>
#include <system_error>
#include <utility>
#include <vector>

#include "access.h"
#include "dvv/base/environment.h"
#include "dvv/base/log.h"
#include "dvv/gpu/gpu_info.h"
#include "dvv/gpu/gpu_registry.h"
#include "gpu_fixture.h"
#include "instance.h"

using dvv::gpu::DeviceId;
using dvv::gpu::GpuInfo;
using dvv::gpu::GpuRegistry;
using dvv::gpu::test::GpuTest;

namespace {

// Captures the log at the given level for one test; afterwards main()'s stderr sink and level
// are back.
class CapturedLog {
public:
    explicit CapturedLog(dvv::LogLevel level) {
        dvv::Logger::instance().clearSinks();
        dvv::Logger::instance().addSink(sink_);
        dvv::Logger::instance().setLevel(level);
    }
    CapturedLog(const CapturedLog&) = delete;
    CapturedLog& operator=(const CapturedLog&) = delete;
    CapturedLog(CapturedLog&&) = delete;
    CapturedLog& operator=(CapturedLog&&) = delete;
    ~CapturedLog() {
        dvv::Logger::instance().setLevel(dvv::LogLevel::Warn);
        dvv::Logger::instance().clearSinks();
        dvv::Logger::instance().addSink(std::make_shared<dvv::StderrSink>());
    }

    [[nodiscard]] bool contains(const std::string& text) const {
        const auto lines = sink_->lines();
        return std::ranges::any_of(lines, [&text](const std::string& line) {
            return line.find(text) != std::string::npos;
        });
    }

private:
    std::shared_ptr<dvv::MemorySink> sink_ = std::make_shared<dvv::MemorySink>();
};

// Sets (or, with an empty value, removes) an environment variable for one scope.
class ScopedEnvironment {
public:
    ScopedEnvironment(std::string name, const std::string& value)
        : name_(std::move(name)), old_(dvv::getEnvironment(name_)) {
        REQUIRE(dvv::setEnvironment(name_, value));
    }
    ScopedEnvironment(const ScopedEnvironment&) = delete;
    ScopedEnvironment& operator=(const ScopedEnvironment&) = delete;
    ScopedEnvironment(ScopedEnvironment&&) = delete;
    ScopedEnvironment& operator=(ScopedEnvironment&&) = delete;
    ~ScopedEnvironment() { static_cast<void>(dvv::setEnvironment(name_, old_.value_or(""))); }

private:
    std::string name_;
    std::optional<std::string> old_;
};

// A new directory under the system's temporary directory, removed with its content afterwards.
class TemporaryDirectory {
public:
    TemporaryDirectory() {
        static std::atomic<int> counter{0};
        path_ = std::filesystem::temp_directory_path() /
                std::format("dvv-gpu-test-{}-{}",
                            std::chrono::steady_clock::now().time_since_epoch().count(),
                            counter.fetch_add(1));
        std::filesystem::create_directories(path_);
    }
    TemporaryDirectory(const TemporaryDirectory&) = delete;
    TemporaryDirectory& operator=(const TemporaryDirectory&) = delete;
    TemporaryDirectory(TemporaryDirectory&&) = delete;
    TemporaryDirectory& operator=(TemporaryDirectory&&) = delete;
    ~TemporaryDirectory() {
        std::error_code ignored;
        std::filesystem::remove_all(path_, ignored);
    }

    [[nodiscard]] const std::filesystem::path& path() const noexcept { return path_; }

private:
    std::filesystem::path path_;
};

#if defined(_WIN32)
constexpr char MISSING_LAYER_LIBRARY[] = "./dvv_missing_validation_layer.dll";
#else
constexpr char MISSING_LAYER_LIBRARY[] = "./libdvv_missing_validation_layer.so";
#endif

}  // namespace

TEST_CASE_FIXTURE(GpuTest, "the registry finds a usable Vulkan 1.4 device and ranks it first") {
    CHECK(registry().loaderVersion() >= VK_API_VERSION_1_3);
    REQUIRE_FALSE(registry().devices().empty());
    REQUIRE(registry().rankedDevices().size() == registry().devices().size());
    CHECK(registry().rankedDevices().front() == device());

    for (std::uint32_t index = 0; index < registry().devices().size(); ++index) {
        CHECK(registry().devices()[index].id == DeviceId(index));
    }
    const GpuInfo& best = registry().devices()[device().index()];
    INFO(best.name);
    CHECK(best.usable());
    CHECK(best.apiVersion >= VK_API_VERSION_1_4);
    CHECK_FALSE(best.name.empty());
    CHECK_FALSE(best.driverName.empty());
    CHECK(best.deviceLocalMemoryBytes > 0);
    CHECK(std::ranges::any_of(best.deviceUuid, [](std::uint8_t byte) { return byte != 0; }));
}

TEST_CASE_FIXTURE(GpuTest, "the registry validates as DVV_VK_VALIDATION asks") {
    const auto requested = dvv::getEnvironment("DVV_VK_VALIDATION");
    if (!requested || requested->empty()) {
        MESSAGE("DVV_VK_VALIDATION is not set");
        return;
    }
    // The test environment points the loader at the prefix's layer, so it is always found.
    CHECK(dvv::gpu::validationModeName(registry().validation()) == *requested);
}

TEST_CASE("debug messages are counted by the messenger policy and logged") {
    using Severity = vk::DebugUtilsMessageSeverityFlagBitsEXT;
    using Type = vk::DebugUtilsMessageTypeFlagBitsEXT;

    const auto registry = GpuRegistry::create().value();
    const auto& instance = dvv::gpu::detail::Access::instance(*registry);
    REQUIRE(instance.debugUtils());
    const CapturedLog log(dvv::LogLevel::Info);

    const auto submit = [&instance](Severity severity, Type type, const char* text) {
        const vk::DebugUtilsMessengerCallbackDataEXT data{.pMessageIdName = "dvv-test",
                                                          .pMessage = text};
        instance.instance().submitDebugUtilsMessageEXT(
            severity, vk::DebugUtilsMessageTypeFlagsEXT(type), data, instance.dispatch());
    };
    const std::uint64_t before = registry->validationMessageCount();
    submit(Severity::eError, Type::eGeneral, "counted general error");
    submit(Severity::eWarning, Type::eValidation, "counted validation warning");
    submit(Severity::eWarning, Type::eGeneral, "uncounted general warning");
    submit(Severity::eInfo, Type::eValidation, "uncounted validation info");
    CHECK(registry->validationMessageCount() - before == 2);
    CHECK(log.contains("Vulkan general error [dvv-test]: counted general error"));
    CHECK(log.contains("Vulkan validation warning [dvv-test]: counted validation warning"));
    CHECK(log.contains("Vulkan general warning [dvv-test]: uncounted general warning"));
    // Logged at Debug, below the Info level set here.
    CHECK_FALSE(log.contains("uncounted validation info"));
}

TEST_CASE("a deliberate validation error is counted") {
    const auto registry = GpuRegistry::create().value();
    if (registry->validation() == dvv::gpu::ValidationMode::Off) {
        MESSAGE("validation is off; nothing to check");
        return;
    }
    const auto& instance = dvv::gpu::detail::Access::instance(*registry);
    const vk::PhysicalDevice physical =
        instance.physicalDevices().at(registry->bestDevice().value_or(DeviceId(0)).index()).handle;
    const CapturedLog log(dvv::LogLevel::Error);
    // An image usage of 0 is invalid usage (VkPhysicalDeviceImageFormatInfo2::usage must not be 0).
    const vk::PhysicalDeviceImageFormatInfo2 info{.format = vk::Format::eR8G8B8A8Unorm,
                                                  .type = vk::ImageType::e2D,
                                                  .tiling = vk::ImageTiling::eOptimal};
    static_cast<void>(physical.getImageFormatProperties2(info, instance.dispatch()));
    CHECK(registry->validationMessageCount() > 0);
    CHECK(log.contains("Vulkan validation error"));
}

TEST_CASE("a validation layer that cannot be loaded turns validation off without a count") {
    // A manifest for the validation layer whose library does not exist, as the only layer the
    // loader sees. vkCreateInstance fails (VK_ERROR_LAYER_NOT_PRESENT from the Linux loader 1.3,
    // VK_ERROR_OUT_OF_HOST_MEMORY from the Windows loader 1.4), and the loader reports the failed
    // library load at ERROR through the instance-creation messenger, which counts it.
    const TemporaryDirectory folder;
    {
        std::ofstream manifest(folder.path() / "VkLayer_khronos_validation.json");
        manifest << R"({"file_format_version": "1.2.0", "layer": {)"
                 << R"("name": "VK_LAYER_KHRONOS_validation", "type": "GLOBAL", )"
                 << R"("library_path": ")" << MISSING_LAYER_LIBRARY << R"(", )"
                 << R"("api_version": "1.4.365", "implementation_version": "1", )"
                 << R"("description": "dvv-gpu test layer without a library"}})";
    }
    const ScopedEnvironment layer_path("VK_LAYER_PATH", folder.path().string());
    const ScopedEnvironment add_layer_path("VK_ADD_LAYER_PATH", "");

    {
        const CapturedLog log(dvv::LogLevel::Warn);
        const auto registry = GpuRegistry::create(
            {.validation = dvv::gpu::ValidationMode::On, .validationRequired = false});
        if (!registry) {
            FAIL(registry.error().describe());
        }
        CHECK((*registry)->validation() == dvv::gpu::ValidationMode::Off);
        CHECK((*registry)->validationMessageCount() == 0);
        CHECK(log.contains("VK_LAYER_KHRONOS_validation could not be loaded; validation is off"));
    }

    const auto required = GpuRegistry::create(
        {.validation = dvv::gpu::ValidationMode::On, .validationRequired = true});
    REQUIRE_FALSE(required.has_value());
    CHECK((required.error().code == dvv::Errc::Unsupported ||
           required.error().code == dvv::Errc::OutOfMemory));
}
```

`dvv-gpu/tests/run-gpu-info.cmake`:

```cmake
# Runs dvv-gpu-info and checks its text and JSON output, its exit codes and that no Vulkan
# validation message was logged. Exits with 77 (CTest: skipped) when the tool finds no usable
# device, unless DVV_REQUIRE_GPU=1. Called by the dvv_gpu.gpu_info test with -DTOOL=<executable>.
cmake_minimum_required(VERSION 4.2)

if(NOT DEFINED TOOL)
    message(FATAL_ERROR "run-gpu-info.cmake needs -DTOOL")
endif()

# run(<expected exit code> <output variable> <arguments>...)
function(run expected output_variable)
    execute_process(COMMAND "${TOOL}" ${ARGN}
                    RESULT_VARIABLE status OUTPUT_VARIABLE output ERROR_VARIABLE errors)
    if(status EQUAL 4 AND NOT expected EQUAL 4)
        if("$ENV{DVV_REQUIRE_GPU}" STREQUAL "1")
            message(FATAL_ERROR "dvv-gpu-info found no usable device (DVV_REQUIRE_GPU=1):\n"
                                "${output}${errors}")
        endif()
        message(STATUS "dvv-gpu-info found no usable device; skipped\n${output}${errors}")
        cmake_language(EXIT 77)
    endif()
    if(NOT status EQUAL expected)
        message(FATAL_ERROR "dvv-gpu-info ${ARGN}: exit ${status}, expected ${expected}\n"
                            "${output}${errors}")
    endif()
    # Counted debug messages are logged as warnings or errors, which the tool prints to stderr.
    if(errors MATCHES "Vulkan [a-z|-]+ (warning|error)")
        message(FATAL_ERROR "dvv-gpu-info ${ARGN} logged a Vulkan validation message:\n${errors}")
    endif()
    set(${output_variable} "${output}" PARENT_SCOPE)
endfunction()

run(0 json --json)
string(JSON selected GET "${json}" selectedDevice)
string(JSON first GET "${json}" devices 0 id)
string(JSON usable GET "${json}" devices 0 usable)
string(JSON api_version GET "${json}" devices 0 apiVersion)
string(JSON validation GET "${json}" validation)
if(NOT selected STREQUAL first OR NOT usable)
    message(FATAL_ERROR "the selected device must be usable and ranked first:\n${json}")
endif()
if(NOT api_version MATCHES "^1\\.([4-9]|[1-9][0-9])\\.")
    message(FATAL_ERROR "the selected device reports Vulkan ${api_version}:\n${json}")
endif()
if(DEFINED ENV{DVV_VK_VALIDATION} AND NOT "$ENV{DVV_VK_VALIDATION}" STREQUAL ""
   AND NOT validation STREQUAL "$ENV{DVV_VK_VALIDATION}")
    message(FATAL_ERROR "validation is '${validation}', DVV_VK_VALIDATION asks for "
                        "'$ENV{DVV_VK_VALIDATION}'")
endif()

run(0 text)
if(NOT text MATCHES "Device ${selected}: [^\n]+, selected\n")
    message(FATAL_ERROR "the text output does not mark device ${selected} as selected:\n${text}")
endif()

run(1 usage --no-such-option)
run(0 help --help)
if(NOT help MATCHES "usage: dvv-gpu-info")
    message(FATAL_ERROR "--help prints no usage:\n${help}")
endif()
```

### Task 16: ExecutionContext with device, queue, allocator and retire queue

**Files:**

- Consumes (dvv-base): `Managed<T>` (`template <class F> explicit Managed(CreationKey<F>&& key) noexcept`), `ManagedObject::objectId()`, `ownerId()`, `typeName()`, `dvv::ObjectId`, `CreationKey<F>`, `LifetimeRegistry::instance().issue<T>(ObjectId owner = NO_OWNER, std::source_location = current()) -> LifetimeToken`, `ManagedType<T>`, `makeError(Errc, std::string, std::source_location)`, `Error::describe()`, `Error::code`, `DVV_TRY`, `DVV_TRY_VOID`, `DVV_LOG_INFO`, `DVV_LOG_ERROR`.
- Produces (C++, public, `dvv/gpu/execution_context.h`, namespace `dvv::gpu`): `struct ContextOptions {};` (empty; the pipeline cache adds its file); `struct RetireStats { std::size_t retiring = 0; std::uint64_t retired = 0; std::uint64_t freed = 0; };`; `class ExecutionContext final : public Managed<ExecutionContext>, public std::enable_shared_from_this<ExecutionContext>` with constructor `ExecutionContext(CreationKey<GpuRegistry> key, std::shared_ptr<GpuRegistry> registry, std::shared_ptr<detail::DeviceCore> core) noexcept`, destructor (drains; logs a failure), `[[nodiscard]] const std::shared_ptr<GpuRegistry>& registry() const noexcept`, `[[nodiscard]] DeviceId deviceId() const noexcept`, `[[nodiscard]] DeviceGeneration generation() const noexcept`, `[[nodiscard]] const GpuInfo& deviceInfo() const noexcept`, `[[nodiscard]] Result<void> drain()`, `[[nodiscard]] RetireStats retireStats() const noexcept`. In `dvv/gpu/gpu_registry.h` (which now includes `execution_context.h`): `[[nodiscard]] Result<std::shared_ptr<ExecutionContext>> GpuRegistry::createContext(DeviceId id, const ContextOptions& options = {})` - `Errc::Internal` for an id that does not exist, `Errc::Unsupported` for a device that misses requirements, converted Vulkan errors otherwise; the context's owner in the lifetime registry is the registry.
- Produces (C++, private, `src/device_core.h`, namespace `dvv::gpu::detail`): `struct RetiredObject { std::uint64_t timelineValue = 0; std::variant<vk::Buffer, vk::Image> handle{}; VmaAllocation allocation = nullptr; };` and `class DeviceCore` with `[[nodiscard]] static Result<std::shared_ptr<DeviceCore>> create(std::shared_ptr<const GpuRegistry> registry, const InstanceCore& instance, const PhysicalDevice& physical, const GpuInfo& info)`, accessors `id()`, `generation()`, `info()`, `physicalDevice() -> vk::PhysicalDevice`, `device() -> vk::Device`, `dispatch() -> const vk::detail::DispatchLoaderDynamic&`, `allocator() -> VmaAllocator`, `queueFamily() -> std::uint32_t`, `queue() -> vk::Queue`, `timeline() -> vk::Semaphore` (all `const noexcept`), `[[nodiscard]] std::unique_lock<std::mutex> lockQueue()`, `[[nodiscard]] std::uint64_t reserveSubmitValue() noexcept` (the next timeline value, reserved before the submission's commands are recorded), `void setSubmitted(std::uint64_t value, const std::unique_lock<std::mutex>& queue_lock) noexcept` (records that the signal of `value` is on the queue; asserts that `queue_lock` owns the queue mutex), `[[nodiscard]] std::uint64_t lastReservedValue() const noexcept`, `[[nodiscard]] std::uint64_t lastSubmittedValue() const noexcept` (both 0 before the first submission), `[[nodiscard]] Result<std::uint64_t> completedValue() const`, `void retire(std::variant<vk::Buffer, vk::Image> handle, VmaAllocation allocation) noexcept`, `std::size_t collect() noexcept`, `[[nodiscard]] Result<void> drain()`, `[[nodiscard]] RetireStats retireStats() const noexcept`, `template <class Handle> void setDebugName(Handle handle, const std::string& name) const noexcept`. In `src/access.h`: `[[nodiscard]] static DeviceCore& Access::core(const ExecutionContext& context) noexcept`.
- Produces (tests): `[[nodiscard]] ExecutionContext& GpuTest::context()` (a context on `device()`, created on first use; the fixture's destructor destroys it before the validation check); compile-fail case `dvv_gpu.compile_fail.3` (an `ExecutionContext` constructed without a key: MSVC `C2661`, Clang `no matching constructor`).
- Behaviour: the device is created with every required feature (`enabledFeatures`) and `VK_EXT_memory_budget` when the device has it, one queue from the universal family, its own `DispatchLoaderDynamic`, a VMA allocator (dynamic functions, `BUFFER_DEVICE_ADDRESS`, `EXT_MEMORY_BUDGET` when present, Vulkan 1.4) and a timeline semaphore starting at 0, all named for debuggers (`dvv device <id> generation <n>`, `... queue`, `... timeline`). The retire queue tags an object with `lastReservedValue()` (any work that used it was submitted, or is being recorded, no later); `collect()` frees what the timeline has reached; `drain()` reads the last submitted value under the queue lock, waits until the device is idle and frees everything up to that value (objects tagged with a value that is reserved but not yet submitted stay, because the commands being recorded may use them). Contract for the submit thread (implemented by the `Submitter` of Task 18): only it reserves values, it reserves before recording, and every reserved value is signalled on the queue, in order, also when recording or submitting fails (then by a batch with the signal alone), and reported with `setSubmitted()` under the queue lock; a reserved value that is never signalled keeps its retired objects until `drain()` or the core's destruction. `DeviceCore`'s destructor waits idle, frees every retired object, then destroys the timeline, the allocator and the device; it keeps the registry (and with it the instance) alive.

Note: `DeviceCore` is an implementation part shared by the context and, from Task 17 on, its resources (like `InstanceCore`, it is not registered with the lifetime registry; the managed objects that hold it are). The device is created with dvv-gpu's own Vulkan-Hpp code, not with vk-bootstrap, so `createContext` does not take `vkBootstrapMutex()`. Nothing submits work yet: `lockQueue()`, `reserveSubmitValue()` and `setSubmitted()` are the hooks for the submit thread of Task 18, and the tests stand in for a submission by reserving a value, reporting it submitted and signalling the timeline from the host. The GPU tests run on the local GPU (Windows) and on lavapipe (WSL); every one ends with a validation message count of zero.

Note (review fixes): retired objects are tagged with the last *reserved* value, not the last submitted one. The submit thread records the commands of submission N before it submits N; tagging with the last submitted value (N-1) would let `collect()` free an object released during that window while submission N still uses it. Hence `reserveSubmitValue()` (before recording) and `setSubmitted()` (after the submission, under the queue lock) replace a single "next submit value" call, and the reserved-value contract above makes sure every reserved value is reached. `setSubmitted()` asserts in Debug builds that its lock argument owns the queue mutex. The case "a device that misses requirements gets no device" calls `DeviceCore::create` white-box with a copy of the usable device's `GpuInfo` that lists a missing requirement, so the `Errc::Unsupported` path runs on every machine (every device the plan targets is usable).

Every Windows command below that runs the GPU tests sets `DVV_VK_VALIDATION=sync` itself, in the same command, as in Task 15 (the tool does not keep environment variables between calls), and removes it afterwards.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

`dvv-gpu/tests/gpu_test_context.cpp` (the retire-queue tests make raw VMA buffers, so the GPU test executable now links VMA):

```cpp
#include <doctest/doctest.h>

#include <cstdint>
#include <memory>
#include <utility>

#include "access.h"
#include "device_core.h"
#include "dvv/base/error.h"
#include "dvv/base/lifetime.h"
#include "dvv/gpu/execution_context.h"
#include "dvv/gpu/gpu_registry.h"
#include "gpu_fixture.h"

using dvv::gpu::DeviceId;
using dvv::gpu::ExecutionContext;
using dvv::gpu::detail::Access;
using dvv::gpu::test::GpuTest;

namespace {

// A buffer made directly through VMA, outside GpuBuffer, to exercise the retire queue alone.
vk::Buffer rawBuffer(dvv::gpu::detail::DeviceCore& core, VmaAllocation& allocation) {
    const vk::BufferCreateInfo info{.size = 256,
                                    .usage = vk::BufferUsageFlagBits::eStorageBuffer,
                                    .sharingMode = vk::SharingMode::eExclusive};
    VmaAllocationCreateInfo allocation_info{};
    allocation_info.usage = VMA_MEMORY_USAGE_AUTO;
    VkBuffer buffer = VK_NULL_HANDLE;
    REQUIRE(vmaCreateBuffer(core.allocator(), &static_cast<const VkBufferCreateInfo&>(info),
                            &allocation_info, &buffer, &allocation, nullptr) == VK_SUCCESS);
    return vk::Buffer(buffer);
}

}  // namespace

TEST_CASE_FIXTURE(GpuTest, "a context runs on the chosen device in its first generation") {
    ExecutionContext& ctx = context();
    CHECK(ctx.deviceId() == device());
    CHECK(ctx.generation() == dvv::gpu::FIRST_GENERATION);
    CHECK(ctx.deviceInfo().name == registry().devices()[device().index()].name);
    CHECK(ctx.registry().get() == &registry());
    CHECK(ctx.ownerId() == registry().objectId());
    CHECK(ctx.typeName() == "dvv::gpu::ExecutionContext");

    const auto stats = ctx.retireStats();
    CHECK(stats.retiring == 0);
    CHECK(stats.retired == 0);
    CHECK(stats.freed == 0);
    CHECK(ctx.drain().has_value());
}

TEST_CASE_FIXTURE(GpuTest, "createContext rejects a device that does not exist") {
    const auto missing =
        registry().createContext(DeviceId(static_cast<std::uint32_t>(registry().devices().size())));
    REQUIRE_FALSE(missing.has_value());
    CHECK(missing.error().code == dvv::Errc::Internal);
}

TEST_CASE_FIXTURE(GpuTest, "a device that misses requirements gets no device") {
    // White-box: the usable device as if it lacked a required feature.
    dvv::gpu::GpuInfo info = registry().devices()[device().index()];
    info.missingRequirements = {"maintenance6"};
    const auto& instance = Access::instance(registry());
    const auto core =
        dvv::gpu::detail::DeviceCore::create(registry().shared_from_this(), instance,
                                             instance.physicalDevices()[device().index()], info);
    REQUIRE_FALSE(core.has_value());
    CHECK(core.error().code == dvv::Errc::Unsupported);
}

TEST_CASE_FIXTURE(GpuTest, "retired objects are freed once the timeline reaches their value") {
    auto& core = Access::core(context());
    VmaAllocation first_allocation = nullptr;
    const vk::Buffer first = rawBuffer(core, first_allocation);

    // Nothing submitted yet: tagged 0, which the timeline has already reached.
    core.retire(first, first_allocation);
    CHECK(context().retireStats().retiring == 1);
    CHECK(core.collect() == 1);
    CHECK(context().retireStats().retiring == 0);

    // As if a submission signalling 1 were being recorded: what is retired now waits for it.
    CHECK(core.reserveSubmitValue() == 1);
    CHECK(core.lastReservedValue() == 1);
    CHECK(core.lastSubmittedValue() == 0);
    VmaAllocation second_allocation = nullptr;
    const vk::Buffer second = rawBuffer(core, second_allocation);
    core.retire(second, second_allocation);
    CHECK(core.collect() == 0);
    CHECK(context().retireStats().retiring == 1);
    {
        const auto lock = core.lockQueue();
        core.setSubmitted(1, lock);
    }
    CHECK(core.lastSubmittedValue() == 1);

    // The "submission" completes.
    const std::uint64_t one = 1;
    const vk::SemaphoreSignalInfo signal{.semaphore = core.timeline(), .value = one};
    REQUIRE(core.device().signalSemaphore(signal, core.dispatch()) == vk::Result::eSuccess);
    CHECK(core.completedValue().value() == 1);
    CHECK(core.collect() == 1);

    const auto stats = context().retireStats();
    CHECK(stats.retiring == 0);
    CHECK(stats.retired == 2);
    CHECK(stats.freed == 2);
}

TEST_CASE_FIXTURE(GpuTest, "drain frees everything retired after waiting for the device") {
    auto& core = Access::core(context());
    {
        const std::uint64_t value = core.reserveSubmitValue();
        const auto lock = core.lockQueue();
        core.setSubmitted(value, lock);  // never signalled
    }
    VmaAllocation allocation = nullptr;
    const vk::Buffer buffer = rawBuffer(core, allocation);
    core.retire(buffer, allocation);
    CHECK(core.collect() == 0);
    // The device is idle, so nothing can still use the buffer.
    REQUIRE(context().drain().has_value());
    CHECK(context().retireStats().retiring == 0);
    CHECK(context().retireStats().freed == 1);
}

TEST_CASE("a context keeps its registry alive") {
    auto registry = dvv::gpu::GpuRegistry::create().value();
    const dvv::ObjectId registry_id = registry->objectId();
    std::shared_ptr<ExecutionContext> ctx =
        registry->createContext(registry->rankedDevices().front()).value();  // main() checked it
    const std::weak_ptr<dvv::gpu::GpuRegistry> watch = registry;
    registry.reset();
    CHECK_FALSE(watch.expired());
    CHECK(ctx->registry()->objectId() == registry_id);
    CHECK(ctx->drain().has_value());
    CHECK(ctx->registry()->validationMessageCount() == 0);
    ctx.reset();
    CHECK(watch.expired());
}
```

```cpp
#pragma once

#include <memory>

#include "dvv/gpu/device_id.h"
#include "dvv/gpu/execution_context.h"
#include "dvv/gpu/gpu_registry.h"

namespace dvv::gpu::test {

// Base of every GPU test (TEST_CASE_FIXTURE): a registry configured from the environment
// (DVV_VK_VALIDATION, DVV_VK_VALIDATION_REQUIRED) and, on first use, a context on the best device.
// At the end of the test the context is destroyed first (destroying a device reports leaked
// Vulkan objects), then no validation message may have been counted.
class GpuTest {
public:
    GpuTest();
    GpuTest(const GpuTest&) = delete;
    GpuTest& operator=(const GpuTest&) = delete;
    GpuTest(GpuTest&&) = delete;
    GpuTest& operator=(GpuTest&&) = delete;
    ~GpuTest();

    [[nodiscard]] GpuRegistry& registry() const noexcept { return *registry_; }
    // The best usable device (main() checked that one exists).
    [[nodiscard]] DeviceId device() const;
    // A context on device(), created on first use.
    [[nodiscard]] ExecutionContext& context();

private:
    std::shared_ptr<GpuRegistry> registry_;
    std::shared_ptr<ExecutionContext> context_;
};

}  // namespace dvv::gpu::test
```

`dvv-gpu/tests/gpu_fixture.cpp` (replace the whole file):

```cpp
#include "gpu_fixture.h"

#include <doctest/doctest.h>

#include <utility>

namespace dvv::gpu::test {

GpuTest::GpuTest() {
    auto registry = GpuRegistry::create();
    if (!registry) {
        FAIL(registry.error().describe());
    }
    registry_ = std::move(*registry);
}

GpuTest::~GpuTest() {
    context_.reset();
    CHECK(registry_->validationMessageCount() == 0);
}

DeviceId GpuTest::device() const {
    if (const auto best = registry_->bestDevice()) {
        return *best;
    }
    FAIL("no usable Vulkan device");
    return DeviceId(0);  // not reached: FAIL throws
}

ExecutionContext& GpuTest::context() {
    if (!context_) {
        auto context = registry_->createContext(device());
        if (!context) {
            FAIL(context.error().describe());
        }
        context_ = std::move(*context);
    }
    return *context_;
}

}  // namespace dvv::gpu::test
```

```cpp
// Compile-fail cases for dvv-gpu's managed types (spec §9.4). DVV_COMPILE_FAIL_CASE selects one:
//   0  control: a registry created through its factory compiles
//   1  constructing a CreationKey<GpuRegistry> outside the registry
//   2  constructing a GpuRegistry without a key
//   3  constructing an ExecutionContext without a key
#include <memory>

#include "dvv/base/lifetime.h"
#include "dvv/gpu/execution_context.h"
#include "dvv/gpu/gpu_registry.h"

int main() {
    const auto registry = dvv::gpu::GpuRegistry::create();
#if DVV_COMPILE_FAIL_CASE == 1
    dvv::CreationKey<dvv::gpu::GpuRegistry> forged(
        dvv::LifetimeRegistry::instance().issue<dvv::gpu::GpuRegistry>());
#elif DVV_COMPILE_FAIL_CASE == 2
    dvv::gpu::GpuRegistry direct(std::unique_ptr<dvv::gpu::detail::InstanceCore>{});
#elif DVV_COMPILE_FAIL_CASE == 3
    dvv::gpu::ExecutionContext direct(*registry, std::shared_ptr<dvv::gpu::detail::DeviceCore>{});
#endif
    return registry.has_value() ? 0 : 1;
}
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Run the tests and see them fail**

Windows workflows of dvv-gpu take several minutes (the prototype's `vs2026` run took about 8 minutes, most of it in the package test): use a 10-minute tool timeout or run them in the background (`run_in_background`). The GPU tests run with synchronization validation; every Windows command sets `DVV_VK_VALIDATION=sync` itself, as in Task 15.

```powershell
$env:DVV_VK_VALIDATION = 'sync'; Push-Location dvv-gpu; cmake --workflow --preset vs2026; Pop-Location; Remove-Item Env:DVV_VK_VALIDATION
```

Expected: FAIL to compile: `gpu_fixture.cpp`, `gpu_test_registry.cpp` and `direct_construction.cpp` (the control `dvv_gpu_compile_ok`) with `C1083: Cannot open include file: 'dvv/gpu/execution_context.h': No such file or directory`, `gpu_test_context.cpp` with `C1083: Cannot open include file: 'device_core.h'`.

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

`dvv-gpu/include/dvv/gpu/execution_context.h`:

```cpp
#pragma once

#include <cstddef>
#include <cstdint>
#include <memory>

#include "dvv/base/error.h"
#include "dvv/base/lifetime.h"
#include "dvv/gpu/device_id.h"
#include "dvv/gpu/gpu_info.h"

namespace dvv::gpu {

class GpuRegistry;

namespace detail {
class DeviceCore;
struct Access;
}  // namespace detail

// Settings for GpuRegistry::createContext(). None yet; the pipeline cache adds its file.
struct ContextOptions {};

// Counters of the retire queue (spec §5.3, §9.4).
struct RetireStats {
    std::size_t retiring = 0;   // handles waiting until the GPU no longer uses them
    std::uint64_t retired = 0;  // handles ever queued
    std::uint64_t freed = 0;    // handles ever destroyed
};

// One Vulkan device and everything that works on it (spec §5.2): the device with the required
// features, one universal queue (graphics, compute and transfer; access serialised by a mutex),
// the VMA allocator, the timeline semaphore and the retire queue. Components reach the GPU only
// through a context. Created by GpuRegistry::createContext(); keeps its registry alive.
class ExecutionContext final : public Managed<ExecutionContext>,
                               public std::enable_shared_from_this<ExecutionContext> {
public:
    ExecutionContext(CreationKey<GpuRegistry> key, std::shared_ptr<GpuRegistry> registry,
                     std::shared_ptr<detail::DeviceCore> core) noexcept;
    // Waits until the device is idle and frees everything retired. Resources that are still alive
    // keep the device until they are destroyed.
    ~ExecutionContext();

    [[nodiscard]] const std::shared_ptr<GpuRegistry>& registry() const noexcept {
        return registry_;
    }
    [[nodiscard]] DeviceId deviceId() const noexcept;
    // Device generation (spec §5.2); FIRST_GENERATION until device-lost recovery exists.
    [[nodiscard]] DeviceGeneration generation() const noexcept;
    [[nodiscard]] const GpuInfo& deviceInfo() const noexcept;

    // Waits until the device is idle, then frees everything retired.
    [[nodiscard]] Result<void> drain();
    [[nodiscard]] RetireStats retireStats() const noexcept;

private:
    friend struct detail::Access;

    std::shared_ptr<GpuRegistry> registry_;
    std::shared_ptr<detail::DeviceCore> core_;
};

}  // namespace dvv::gpu
```

`dvv-gpu/src/device_core.h`:

```cpp
#pragma once

// The device behind an ExecutionContext (spec §5.2, §5.3): device, universal queue, VMA allocator,
// timeline semaphore and retire queue. Shared by the context and every resource it created, so the
// device stays alive until the last deferred destruction is done. An internal part of
// ExecutionContext, not registered separately: the managed objects that hold it are.

#include <atomic>
#include <cstddef>
#include <cstdint>
#include <memory>
#include <mutex>
#include <string>
#include <variant>
#include <vector>

#include <vk_mem_alloc.h>

#include "dvv/base/error.h"
#include "dvv/gpu/device_id.h"
#include "dvv/gpu/execution_context.h"
#include "dvv/gpu/gpu_info.h"
#include "dvv/gpu/gpu_registry.h"
#include "vk.h"

namespace dvv::gpu::detail {

class InstanceCore;
struct PhysicalDevice;

// A handle waiting until the GPU no longer uses it, with its memory.
struct RetiredObject {
    std::uint64_t timelineValue = 0;  // freed once the timeline semaphore reaches this value
    std::variant<vk::Buffer, vk::Image> handle{};
    VmaAllocation allocation = nullptr;
};

class DeviceCore {
public:
    // Creates the device with the required features (and the supported optional ones), its
    // universal queue, the VMA allocator (buffer device addresses; memory budget when present) and
    // the timeline semaphore. Keeps the registry, and with it the instance, alive.
    [[nodiscard]] static Result<std::shared_ptr<DeviceCore>> create(
        std::shared_ptr<const GpuRegistry> registry, const InstanceCore& instance,
        const PhysicalDevice& physical, const GpuInfo& info);

    DeviceCore(const DeviceCore&) = delete;
    DeviceCore& operator=(const DeviceCore&) = delete;
    DeviceCore(DeviceCore&&) = delete;
    DeviceCore& operator=(DeviceCore&&) = delete;
    // Waits until the device is idle, frees everything retired, then destroys the timeline, the
    // allocator and the device.
    ~DeviceCore();

    [[nodiscard]] DeviceId id() const noexcept { return id_; }
    [[nodiscard]] DeviceGeneration generation() const noexcept { return generation_; }
    [[nodiscard]] const GpuInfo& info() const noexcept { return info_; }
    [[nodiscard]] vk::PhysicalDevice physicalDevice() const noexcept { return physical_; }
    [[nodiscard]] vk::Device device() const noexcept { return device_; }
    [[nodiscard]] const vk::detail::DispatchLoaderDynamic& dispatch() const noexcept {
        return dispatch_;
    }
    [[nodiscard]] VmaAllocator allocator() const noexcept { return allocator_; }
    [[nodiscard]] std::uint32_t queueFamily() const noexcept { return queue_family_; }
    [[nodiscard]] vk::Queue queue() const noexcept { return queue_; }
    [[nodiscard]] vk::Semaphore timeline() const noexcept { return timeline_; }

    // Queue access (spec §4): every vkQueue* call and vkDeviceWaitIdle happen under this lock.
    [[nodiscard]] std::unique_lock<std::mutex> lockQueue() {
        return std::unique_lock(queue_mutex_);
    }
    // Reserves the timeline value of the next submission before its commands are recorded, so that
    // everything retired from then on (also while recording) waits for that submission.
    // Contract: only the submit thread reserves values, and every reserved value is signalled,
    // in order, also when recording or submitting fails (then by a batch with the signal alone);
    // otherwise the objects retired for it would wait until drain(). The submitter reports each
    // signal with setSubmitted().
    [[nodiscard]] std::uint64_t reserveSubmitValue() noexcept;
    // Records that a signal of `value` (the latest reserved one) is on the queue. The caller holds
    // the queue lock (checked by an assertion), under which it submitted that signal.
    void setSubmitted(std::uint64_t value, const std::unique_lock<std::mutex>& queue_lock) noexcept;
    // The latest reserved value (0 before the first submission); retired objects wait for it.
    [[nodiscard]] std::uint64_t lastReservedValue() const noexcept {
        return last_reserved_.load(std::memory_order_acquire);
    }
    // The value of the latest submission on the queue (0 before the first one).
    [[nodiscard]] std::uint64_t lastSubmittedValue() const noexcept {
        return last_submitted_.load(std::memory_order_acquire);
    }
    // The value the timeline semaphore has reached.
    [[nodiscard]] Result<std::uint64_t> completedValue() const;

    // Queues a handle and its memory for destruction once the work submitted or being recorded so
    // far is complete.
    void retire(std::variant<vk::Buffer, vk::Image> handle, VmaAllocation allocation) noexcept;
    // Frees the retired objects whose timeline value has been reached; returns how many.
    std::size_t collect() noexcept;
    // Waits until the device is idle, then frees everything retired for submissions made so far.
    [[nodiscard]] Result<void> drain();
    [[nodiscard]] RetireStats retireStats() const noexcept;

    // Names an object for debuggers and validation messages; nothing without VK_EXT_debug_utils.
    template <class Handle>
    void setDebugName(Handle handle, const std::string& name) const noexcept;

    // Use create().
    DeviceCore() = default;

private:
    std::size_t freeRetired(std::uint64_t reached) noexcept;
    void destroy(const RetiredObject& object) const noexcept;
    void setObjectName(vk::ObjectType type, std::uint64_t handle,
                       const std::string& name) const noexcept;

    std::shared_ptr<const GpuRegistry> registry_;
    DeviceId id_{0};
    DeviceGeneration generation_ = FIRST_GENERATION;
    GpuInfo info_;
    bool debug_utils_ = false;
    vk::PhysicalDevice physical_;
    vk::Device device_;
    vk::detail::DispatchLoaderDynamic dispatch_;
    VmaAllocator allocator_ = nullptr;
    std::uint32_t queue_family_ = 0;
    vk::Queue queue_;
    vk::Semaphore timeline_;

    std::mutex queue_mutex_;
    std::atomic<std::uint64_t> last_reserved_{0};
    std::atomic<std::uint64_t> last_submitted_{0};

    mutable std::mutex retire_mutex_;
    std::vector<RetiredObject> retired_;  // not sorted: retire() may race with a submission
    std::uint64_t retired_total_ = 0;
    std::uint64_t freed_total_ = 0;
};

template <class Handle>
void DeviceCore::setDebugName(Handle handle, const std::string& name) const noexcept {
    using CHandle = typename Handle::CType;
    setObjectName(Handle::objectType, reinterpret_cast<std::uint64_t>(static_cast<CHandle>(handle)),
                  name);
}

}  // namespace dvv::gpu::detail
```

`dvv-gpu/src/device_core.cpp`:

```cpp
#include "device_core.h"

#include <algorithm>
#include <cassert>
#include <format>
#include <iterator>
#include <string>
#include <utility>

#include "devices.h"
#include "dvv/base/log.h"
#include "instance.h"
#include "vk_result.h"

namespace dvv::gpu::detail {

Result<std::shared_ptr<DeviceCore>> DeviceCore::create(std::shared_ptr<const GpuRegistry> registry,
                                                       const InstanceCore& instance,
                                                       const PhysicalDevice& physical,
                                                       const GpuInfo& info) {
    if (!info.usable() || !physical.universalQueueFamily) {
        return makeError(
            Errc::Unsupported,
            std::format("device {} ({}) does not meet the requirements", info.id, info.name));
    }
    // Every member is set step by step; the destructor releases whatever exists when a step fails.
    auto core = std::make_shared<DeviceCore>();
    core->registry_ = std::move(registry);
    core->id_ = info.id;
    core->info_ = info;
    core->debug_utils_ = instance.debugUtils();
    core->physical_ = physical.handle;
    core->queue_family_ = *physical.universalQueueFamily;

    const float priority = 1.0F;
    const vk::DeviceQueueCreateInfo queue_info{
        .queueFamilyIndex = core->queue_family_, .queueCount = 1, .pQueuePriorities = &priority};
    const FeatureChain features = enabledFeatures(info.optional);
    std::vector<const char*> extensions;
    if (info.optional.memoryBudget) {
        extensions.push_back(vk::EXTMemoryBudgetExtensionName);
    }
    const vk::DeviceCreateInfo device_info{
        .pNext = &features.get<vk::PhysicalDeviceFeatures2>(),
        .queueCreateInfoCount = 1,
        .pQueueCreateInfos = &queue_info,
        .enabledExtensionCount = static_cast<std::uint32_t>(extensions.size()),
        .ppEnabledExtensionNames = extensions.data()};
    const auto device = core->physical_.createDevice(device_info, nullptr, instance.dispatch());
    DVV_TRY_VOID(vkCheck(device.result, "vkCreateDevice"));
    core->device_ = device.value;
    core->dispatch_.init(
        static_cast<VkInstance>(instance.instance()), instance.dispatch().vkGetInstanceProcAddr,
        static_cast<VkDevice>(core->device_), instance.dispatch().vkGetDeviceProcAddr);
    core->queue_ = core->device_.getQueue(core->queue_family_, 0, core->dispatch_);

    VmaVulkanFunctions functions{};
    functions.vkGetInstanceProcAddr = core->dispatch_.vkGetInstanceProcAddr;
    functions.vkGetDeviceProcAddr = core->dispatch_.vkGetDeviceProcAddr;
    VmaAllocatorCreateInfo allocator_info{};
    allocator_info.flags = VMA_ALLOCATOR_CREATE_BUFFER_DEVICE_ADDRESS_BIT;
    if (info.optional.memoryBudget) {
        allocator_info.flags |= VMA_ALLOCATOR_CREATE_EXT_MEMORY_BUDGET_BIT;
    }
    allocator_info.physicalDevice = core->physical_;
    allocator_info.device = core->device_;
    allocator_info.instance = instance.instance();
    allocator_info.pVulkanFunctions = &functions;
    allocator_info.vulkanApiVersion = VK_API_VERSION_1_4;
    DVV_TRY_VOID(
        vkCheck(vmaCreateAllocator(&allocator_info, &core->allocator_), "vmaCreateAllocator"));

    vk::SemaphoreTypeCreateInfo timeline_type{.semaphoreType = vk::SemaphoreType::eTimeline,
                                              .initialValue = 0};
    const auto timeline =
        core->device_.createSemaphore({.pNext = &timeline_type}, nullptr, core->dispatch_);
    DVV_TRY_VOID(vkCheck(timeline.result, "vkCreateSemaphore"));
    core->timeline_ = timeline.value;

    const std::string label =
        std::format("dvv device {} generation {}", info.id, core->generation_);
    core->setDebugName(core->device_, label);
    core->setDebugName(core->queue_, label + " queue");
    core->setDebugName(core->timeline_, label + " timeline");
    DVV_LOG_INFO("Vulkan device created: {} ({}), Vulkan {}", info.name, info.id,
                 formatVulkanVersion(info.apiVersion));
    return core;
}

DeviceCore::~DeviceCore() {
    if (!device_) {
        return;
    }
    {
        const auto lock = lockQueue();
        const vk::Result idle = device_.waitIdle(dispatch_);
        if (idle != vk::Result::eSuccess) {
            DVV_LOG_ERROR("vkDeviceWaitIdle failed while destroying device {}: {}", id_,
                          vk::to_string(idle));
        }
    }
    freeRetired(UINT64_MAX);
    if (timeline_) {
        device_.destroySemaphore(timeline_, nullptr, dispatch_);
    }
    if (allocator_ != nullptr) {
        vmaDestroyAllocator(allocator_);
    }
    device_.destroy(nullptr, dispatch_);
}

std::uint64_t DeviceCore::reserveSubmitValue() noexcept {
    return last_reserved_.fetch_add(1, std::memory_order_acq_rel) + 1;
}

void DeviceCore::setSubmitted(std::uint64_t value,
                              const std::unique_lock<std::mutex>& queue_lock) noexcept {
    assert(queue_lock.owns_lock() && queue_lock.mutex() == &queue_mutex_);
    static_cast<void>(queue_lock);  // used only by the assertion in Release builds
    last_submitted_.store(value, std::memory_order_release);
}

Result<std::uint64_t> DeviceCore::completedValue() const {
    const auto value = device_.getSemaphoreCounterValue(timeline_, dispatch_);
    DVV_TRY_VOID(vkCheck(value.result, "vkGetSemaphoreCounterValue"));
    return value.value;
}

void DeviceCore::retire(std::variant<vk::Buffer, vk::Image> handle,
                        VmaAllocation allocation) noexcept {
    const std::scoped_lock lock(retire_mutex_);
    // Tagged with the latest reserved submission: any work that used the handle was submitted, or
    // is being recorded, no later.
    retired_.push_back(
        {.timelineValue = lastReservedValue(), .handle = handle, .allocation = allocation});
    ++retired_total_;
}

std::size_t DeviceCore::collect() noexcept {
    const auto value = device_.getSemaphoreCounterValue(timeline_, dispatch_);
    if (value.result != vk::Result::eSuccess) {
        DVV_LOG_ERROR("vkGetSemaphoreCounterValue failed on device {}: {}", id_,
                      vk::to_string(value.result));
        return 0;
    }
    return freeRetired(value.value);
}

Result<void> DeviceCore::drain() {
    std::uint64_t submitted = 0;
    {
        const auto lock = lockQueue();
        submitted = lastSubmittedValue();  // no submission can follow while the lock is held
        DVV_TRY_VOID(vkCheck(device_.waitIdle(dispatch_), "vkDeviceWaitIdle"));
    }
    // Objects tagged with a value that is reserved but not yet submitted may still be used by
    // the commands being recorded for it; they stay.
    freeRetired(submitted);
    return {};
}

RetireStats DeviceCore::retireStats() const noexcept {
    const std::scoped_lock lock(retire_mutex_);
    return {.retiring = retired_.size(), .retired = retired_total_, .freed = freed_total_};
}

std::size_t DeviceCore::freeRetired(std::uint64_t reached) noexcept {
    std::vector<RetiredObject> ready;
    {
        const std::scoped_lock lock(retire_mutex_);
        const auto done = std::ranges::stable_partition(
            retired_,
            [reached](const RetiredObject& object) { return object.timelineValue > reached; });
        ready.assign(std::make_move_iterator(done.begin()), std::make_move_iterator(done.end()));
        retired_.erase(done.begin(), done.end());
        freed_total_ += ready.size();
    }
    for (const RetiredObject& object : ready) {
        destroy(object);
    }
    return ready.size();
}

void DeviceCore::destroy(const RetiredObject& object) const noexcept {
    if (const auto* buffer = std::get_if<vk::Buffer>(&object.handle)) {
        vmaDestroyBuffer(allocator_, static_cast<VkBuffer>(*buffer), object.allocation);
    } else if (const auto* image = std::get_if<vk::Image>(&object.handle)) {
        vmaDestroyImage(allocator_, static_cast<VkImage>(*image), object.allocation);
    }
}

void DeviceCore::setObjectName(vk::ObjectType type, std::uint64_t handle,
                               const std::string& name) const noexcept {
    if (!debug_utils_ || name.empty()) {
        return;
    }
    const vk::DebugUtilsObjectNameInfoEXT info{
        .objectType = type, .objectHandle = handle, .pObjectName = name.c_str()};
    // A missing debug name is not worth failing for.
    static_cast<void>(device_.setDebugUtilsObjectNameEXT(info, dispatch_));
}

}  // namespace dvv::gpu::detail
```

`dvv-gpu/src/execution_context.cpp`:

```cpp
#include "dvv/gpu/execution_context.h"

#include <utility>

#include "device_core.h"
#include "dvv/base/log.h"
#include "dvv/gpu/gpu_registry.h"

namespace dvv::gpu {

static_assert(ManagedType<ExecutionContext>);

ExecutionContext::ExecutionContext(CreationKey<GpuRegistry> key,
                                   std::shared_ptr<GpuRegistry> registry,
                                   std::shared_ptr<detail::DeviceCore> core) noexcept
    : Managed(std::move(key)), registry_(std::move(registry)), core_(std::move(core)) {}

ExecutionContext::~ExecutionContext() {
    if (const auto drained = core_->drain(); !drained) {
        DVV_LOG_ERROR("closing the context of device {} failed: {}", core_->id(),
                      drained.error().describe());
    }
}

DeviceId ExecutionContext::deviceId() const noexcept {
    return core_->id();
}

DeviceGeneration ExecutionContext::generation() const noexcept {
    return core_->generation();
}

const GpuInfo& ExecutionContext::deviceInfo() const noexcept {
    return core_->info();
}

Result<void> ExecutionContext::drain() {
    return core_->drain();
}

RetireStats ExecutionContext::retireStats() const noexcept {
    return core_->retireStats();
}

}  // namespace dvv::gpu
```

```cpp
Result<std::shared_ptr<ExecutionContext>> GpuRegistry::createContext(
    DeviceId id, const ContextOptions& /*options*/) {
    if (id.index() >= devices_.size()) {
        return makeError(Errc::Internal, std::format("there is no Vulkan device {}", id));
    }
    DVV_TRY(auto core, detail::DeviceCore::create(shared_from_this(), *instance_,
                                                  instance_->physicalDevices()[id.index()],
                                                  devices_[id.index()]));
    return std::make_shared<ExecutionContext>(
        CreationKey<GpuRegistry>(LifetimeRegistry::instance().issue<ExecutionContext>(objectId())),
        shared_from_this(), std::move(core));
}
```

- [ ] **Step 7: Commit and privacy lint**

```powershell
git -C dvv-gpu add -A
@'
feat: add ExecutionContext with device, queue, allocator and retire queue

registry->createContext(DeviceId, ContextOptions) creates the device of a usable physical device
with every required feature and the supported optional ones (VK_EXT_memory_budget when present),
one universal queue (graphics, compute, transfer) guarded by a mutex, the VMA allocator (dynamic
functions, buffer device addresses, memory budget) and the timeline semaphore, all with debug
names. The device has its own dispatcher.

The parts live in detail::DeviceCore, shared by the context and later by its resources, so the
device outlives the context until the last deferred destruction is done; DeviceCore keeps the
registry, and with it the instance, alive. The context holds shared_ptr<GpuRegistry>.

Retire queue: retire() tags a handle and its VMA allocation with the last reserved submission value;
reserveSubmitValue() is taken before recording, so every submission that can use the handle is
covered. setSubmitted(value, queue_lock) records a submitted value; a reserved value is always
signalled, also when recording or submitting fails (the submit thread arrives in a later commit).
collect() frees what the timeline semaphore has reached; drain() waits until the device is idle
(under the queue lock) and frees everything retired so far. Counters (retiring, retired, freed) are queryable through retireStats().
The context's destructor drains; DeviceCore's destructor waits idle, frees the rest and destroys
timeline, allocator and device. ContextOptions is empty for now.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
'@ | git -C dvv-gpu commit -F -
```
