### Task 22: Compute demo tests

**Files:**
- Create: `dvv-gpu/tests/gpu_test_compute.cpp`
- Modify: `dvv-gpu/tests/CMakeLists.txt` (the new source in `dvv_gpu_device_tests`)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes (Task 21): `ExecutionContext::createComputePipeline(const EmbeddedShader& shader, std::uint32_t push_constant_size)` returning `Result<std::shared_ptr<ComputePipeline>>`; `void CommandRecorder::dispatch(const ComputePipeline& pipeline, std::span<const std::byte> push_constants, std::uint32_t group_count_x, std::uint32_t group_count_y = 1, std::uint32_t group_count_z = 1)` (which records the automatic global barrier first); the GPU test executable `dvv_gpu_device_tests` with its sources in `tests/CMakeLists.txt` (`gpu_fixture.cpp`, `gpu_main.cpp`, `gpu_test_context.cpp`, `gpu_test_pipeline.cpp`, ...).
- Consumes (Task 20): `[[nodiscard]] const dvv::gpu::EmbeddedShader& test_shaders::addOne() noexcept` (`#include "test_shaders/add_one.h"`): the compute shader `add_one.slang`, `[numthreads(64, 1, 1)]`, which adds 1 to the first `count` 32-bit values of the buffer at `address`; 16 bytes of push constants, laid out as `{ std::uint64_t address; std::uint32_t count; std::uint32_t padding; }`.
- Consumes (Tasks 16-19): `ExecutionContext::createBuffer(BufferDesc)` returning `Result<std::shared_ptr<GpuBuffer>>`; `BufferDesc{.size, .usage, .memory, .name}` with `MemoryUsage::HostReadback`; `GpuBuffer::size()`, `handle()`, `deviceAddress()`, `mapped()`, `flush()`, `invalidate()`; `ExecutionContext::createResourcePool()` returning `Result<std::shared_ptr<ResourcePool>>`; `ResourcePool::acquireBuffer(BufferDesc)` returning `Result<std::shared_ptr<GpuBuffer>>` (a lease: the last reference returns the buffer to the pool, which hands it out again once the GPU has finished every submission made or being recorded at that moment) and `ResourcePool::stats()` returning `ResourcePoolStats{free, leased, created, reused}`; the GPU test harness (`GpuTest`: `context()`, `submitAndWait(RecordFunction)`, which also checks at the end of every test case that no validation message was counted).
- Produces (tests): three GPU test cases in `tests/gpu_test_compute.cpp` (the M1a demo, spec §15): `add_one adds 1 to every value of a buffer`, `a dispatch sees the writes of the dispatch before it`, `a pooled buffer is reused once the GPU has finished with it`. No library, tool or public interface changes.
- Behaviour: add_one reaches its buffer only through the device address in the push constants, so neither the recorder nor synchronization validation can see the access (they track the buffer handle only). The first test proves the round trip and the `count` push constant (the buffer holds 1024 values, 1000 are changed, the 24 past the count keep their value). The second test proves the recorder's automatic global barrier: two dispatches in one submission give +2 everywhere. The third test proves the pool reuse end to end: a pooled buffer returned while its submission is being recorded is not handed out again (the next acquisition creates a new buffer); after the submission has finished it is handed out again with the values the GPU wrote.

Note (design deviations): none of this task's tests needs new library code, so there is no failing-to-compile state: Steps 2 and 3 show that the tests are not part of the executable until they are registered. The dependent-dispatch test cannot fail on lavapipe or with synchronization validation, which does not see device-address accesses (design, spec §16); Step 7 is the optional check that it detects a missing barrier on a discrete GPU. The third test blocks inside its record function on a latch so that the lease is returned while the submission is recorded; this is a test technique (as in `gpu_test_submission.cpp`), not something users do. Its latches are counted down on every path (also when the submission fails before its record function runs, and nothing between the latches can throw), so a failure ends as a doctest failure with its reason, not as a CTest timeout. The push-constant struct is pinned by `static_assert`s on its size and member offsets, because pipeline creation checks only the size of the shader's push-constant block.

Every Windows command below that runs the GPU tests sets `DVV_VK_VALIDATION=sync` itself, in the same command, as in Task 15, and removes it afterwards.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the tests**

`dvv-gpu/tests/gpu_test_compute.cpp`:

```cpp
// The M1a demo (spec §15): a compute shader built by slangc changes a buffer on the GPU.
#include <doctest/doctest.h>

#include <atomic>
#include <cstddef>
#include <cstdint>
#include <cstring>
#include <latch>
#include <memory>
#include <span>
#include <thread>
#include <vector>

#include "dvv/base/error.h"
#include "dvv/gpu/command_recorder.h"
#include "dvv/gpu/compute_pipeline.h"
#include "dvv/gpu/execution_context.h"
#include "dvv/gpu/resource_pool.h"
#include "dvv/gpu/resources.h"
#include "gpu_fixture.h"
#include "test_shaders/add_one.h"

using dvv::gpu::BufferDesc;
using dvv::gpu::CommandRecorder;
using dvv::gpu::ComputePipeline;
using dvv::gpu::GpuBuffer;
using dvv::gpu::MemoryUsage;
using dvv::gpu::test::GpuTest;

namespace {

constexpr std::uint32_t GROUP_SIZE = 64;  // [numthreads(64, 1, 1)] in add_one.slang
constexpr std::uint32_t COUNT = 1000;     // the values add_one changes; not a multiple of 64
constexpr std::uint32_t GROUPS = (COUNT + GROUP_SIZE - 1) / GROUP_SIZE;
// The buffer has room for every invocation of the last group: add_one must leave the values
// past COUNT alone.
constexpr std::uint32_t CAPACITY = GROUPS * GROUP_SIZE;
constexpr std::uint32_t UNTOUCHED = 0xdeadbeefU;

// add_one's push constants: the buffer by device address and the number of values.
struct AddOne {
    std::uint64_t data = 0;
    std::uint32_t count = 0;
    std::uint32_t padding = 0;
};
// Pipeline creation checks only the block's size (16): the offsets pin the order of its members.
static_assert(sizeof(AddOne) == 16);
static_assert(offsetof(AddOne, data) == 0 && offsetof(AddOne, count) == 8);

const BufferDesc VALUES{.size = std::uint64_t{CAPACITY} * sizeof(std::uint32_t),
                        .usage = VK_BUFFER_USAGE_STORAGE_BUFFER_BIT,
                        .memory = MemoryUsage::HostReadback,
                        .name = "add_one values"};

// The buffer's content after `added` add_one dispatches.
std::vector<std::uint32_t> expected(std::uint32_t added) {
    std::vector<std::uint32_t> values(CAPACITY, UNTOUCHED);
    for (std::uint32_t i = 0; i < COUNT; ++i) {
        values[i] = i * 3 + added;
    }
    return values;
}

void write(GpuBuffer& buffer, const std::vector<std::uint32_t>& values) {
    REQUIRE(buffer.size() == values.size() * sizeof(std::uint32_t));
    std::memcpy(buffer.mapped().data(), values.data(), buffer.size());
    REQUIRE(buffer.flush().has_value());
}

std::vector<std::uint32_t> read(GpuBuffer& buffer) {
    REQUIRE(buffer.invalidate().has_value());
    std::vector<std::uint32_t> values(buffer.size() / sizeof(std::uint32_t));
    std::memcpy(values.data(), buffer.mapped().data(), buffer.size());
    return values;
}

// The number of values that differ from `wanted`.
std::size_t mismatches(const std::vector<std::uint32_t>& values,
                       const std::vector<std::uint32_t>& wanted) {
    REQUIRE(values.size() == wanted.size());
    std::size_t count = 0;
    for (std::size_t i = 0; i < values.size(); ++i) {
        count += values[i] != wanted[i] ? 1 : 0;
    }
    return count;
}

// Records add_one over the first COUNT values of the buffer.
void addOne(CommandRecorder& recorder, const ComputePipeline& pipeline, const GpuBuffer& buffer) {
    const AddOne push{.data = buffer.deviceAddress(), .count = COUNT};
    recorder.dispatch(pipeline, std::as_bytes(std::span(&push, 1)), GROUPS);
}

}  // namespace

TEST_CASE_FIXTURE(GpuTest, "add_one adds 1 to every value of a buffer") {
    const auto pipeline =
        context().createComputePipeline(test_shaders::addOne(), sizeof(AddOne)).value();
    const auto buffer = context().createBuffer(VALUES).value();
    write(*buffer, expected(0));

    REQUIRE(submitAndWait([&](CommandRecorder& recorder) {
                addOne(recorder, *pipeline, *buffer);
            }).has_value());
    CHECK(mismatches(read(*buffer), expected(1)) == 0);
}

TEST_CASE_FIXTURE(GpuTest, "a dispatch sees the writes of the dispatch before it") {
    // Both dispatches reach the buffer only through its device address, which neither the
    // recorder nor synchronization validation can follow: only the recorder's automatic barrier
    // between the two dispatches orders them.
    const auto pipeline =
        context().createComputePipeline(test_shaders::addOne(), sizeof(AddOne)).value();
    const auto buffer = context().createBuffer(VALUES).value();
    write(*buffer, expected(0));

    REQUIRE(submitAndWait([&](CommandRecorder& recorder) {
                addOne(recorder, *pipeline, *buffer);
                addOne(recorder, *pipeline, *buffer);
            }).has_value());
    const std::vector<std::uint32_t> values = read(*buffer);
    // Without the barrier, the second dispatch may read values the first has not written yet.
    const std::vector<std::uint32_t> once = expected(1);
    std::size_t incremented_once = 0;
    for (std::size_t i = 0; i < COUNT; ++i) {
        incremented_once += values[i] == once[i] ? 1 : 0;
    }
    INFO("values incremented only once: " << incremented_once);
    CHECK(mismatches(values, expected(2)) == 0);
}

TEST_CASE_FIXTURE(GpuTest, "a pooled buffer is reused once the GPU has finished with it") {
    const auto pipeline =
        context().createComputePipeline(test_shaders::addOne(), sizeof(AddOne)).value();
    const auto pool = context().createResourcePool().value();
    auto lease = pool->acquireBuffer(VALUES).value();
    const VkBuffer handle = lease->handle();
    write(*lease, expected(0));

    // The dispatch uses the buffer by device address only: the pool, not the recorder, keeps the
    // buffer from being handed out again while the GPU may still use it.
    GpuBuffer* const borrowed = lease.get();
    std::latch recording(1);
    std::latch returned(1);
    std::atomic<bool> recorded = false;
    dvv::Result<std::uint64_t> result;
    std::jthread submitting([&] {
        result = submitAndWait([&](CommandRecorder& recorder) {
            addOne(recorder, *pipeline, *borrowed);
            recorded = true;
            recording.count_down();
            returned.wait();  // the test returns the buffer meanwhile
        });
        if (!recorded) {
            recording.count_down();  // the submission failed before recording: do not hang
        }
    });
    recording.wait();
    // Nothing may throw before returned.count_down(): unwinding would join `submitting` while it
    // waits for `returned`. The results are checked after the join.
    lease.reset();  // back in the pool while its submission is being recorded
    const std::size_t free_while_recording = pool->stats().free;
    const auto other = pool->acquireBuffer(VALUES);
    returned.count_down();
    submitting.join();
    REQUIRE(result.has_value());
    CHECK(free_while_recording == 1);
    REQUIRE(other.has_value());
    CHECK((*other)->handle() != handle);

    // The submission has finished: the buffer is handed out again, with what the GPU left in it.
    const auto again = pool->acquireBuffer(VALUES).value();
    CHECK(again->handle() == handle);
    CHECK(mismatches(read(*again), expected(1)) == 0);
    const auto stats = pool->stats();
    CHECK(stats.created == 2);
    CHECK(stats.reused == 1);
    CHECK(stats.leased == 2);
    CHECK(stats.free == 0);

    REQUIRE(submitAndWait([&](CommandRecorder& recorder) {
                addOne(recorder, *pipeline, *again);
            }).has_value());
    CHECK(mismatches(read(*again), expected(2)) == 0);
}
```

Run each line in its own tool call, in order; run lines 1, 2 and 5 in the background (`run_in_background`, then wait for the notification), line 5 only after line 2 has finished: the Linux presets together can run longer than 10 minutes, and `clang-tidy` alone can take about 15 minutes. Git runs from Windows only: each lint line lists the files with `git -C dvv-gpu ls-files` in PowerShell (after `git add -A` (line 3) has finished, so the new file is included; the list and the `wsl` call are on one line because tool calls do not share PowerShell variables) and passes them to `clang-format` or `clang-tidy` in WSL as arguments (`lint` is the script name `$0`, the files are `"$@"`). Expected: `vs2026-asan` `100% tests passed, 0 tests failed out of 12` (no package test); Linux lines read `100% tests passed` (`out of 13` for `ninja-clang` in Debug and Release, `out of 12` for ASan and TSan; the GPU tests run on lavapipe with sync validation required; the TSan run covers the extra thread and the latches of the pool test); `FORMAT-OK` and `LINT-OK` with no diagnostics. If clang-format reports a file, run `clang-format -i` on it and repeat from Step 4 (then this step again in full, including `git -C dvv-gpu add -A`).

- [ ] **Step 9: Commit and privacy lint**

Before the commit `git -C dvv-gpu status --short` lists exactly these two entries (in path order: `M  tests/CMakeLists.txt`, `A  tests/gpu_test_compute.cpp`), and `git -C dvv-gpu diff --stat` prints nothing. The commit adds the two files by name, so a leftover change elsewhere cannot be committed with them:

```powershell
git -C dvv-gpu add tests/gpu_test_compute.cpp tests/CMakeLists.txt
@'
test: add the compute demo tests

The M1a demo (spec §15) as GPU tests in gpu_test_compute.cpp, using the
slangc-built add_one shader with the buffer passed by device address in
the push constants:

- add_one round trip: a host-visible buffer of 1024 values, add_one over
  the first 1000 (16 groups of 64); every one of them is incremented and
  the values past the count stay untouched.
- Dependent dispatches: two add_one dispatches on the same buffer in one
  submission give +2 everywhere. The shader reaches the buffer only by
  its device address, which synchronization validation cannot follow, so
  only the recorder's automatic global barrier orders the two dispatches.
  With that barrier disabled locally (not committed), the test fails on
  NVIDIA hardware with all 1000 values incremented only once, while sync
  validation reports nothing; with the barrier it passes.
- ResourcePool reuse end to end: a pooled buffer returned while its
  dispatch is being recorded is not handed out again (the next
  acquisition creates a new buffer); once the submission has finished
  it is reused with the values the GPU wrote, and serves another
  dispatch.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>
'@ | git -C dvv-gpu commit -F -
```

```powershell
cmake -DREPO=dvv-gpu -DMODE=commits "-DEXTRA_TERMS=$env:USERNAME;$env:COMPUTERNAME" -P rawreel-workspace/tools/privacy-lint.cmake
```

Expected: `-- privacy-lint: clean (commits)`. Do not push; `dvv-gpu` stays a local repository until the publishing task.

**Files:**

- No change: the sibling repositories' `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (their `permissions: contents: read` is enough for the cache actions).

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes (Task 13): `DVV_VK_VALIDATION` (`off`, `on`, `sync`) and `DVV_VK_VALIDATION_REQUIRED` (`0`, `1`) read by `GpuRegistry`. Task 15: the GPU test executables exit 77 without a usable Vulkan 1.4 device (CTest `SKIP_RETURN_CODE 77`) unless `DVV_REQUIRE_GPU=1`. Task 20: dvv-gpu's tests include headers that `dvv_add_shaders` generates at build time (`test_shaders/add_one.h`). Task 3: bootstrap's up-to-date stamp hashes `deps/**`, `cmake/DvvThirdParty.cmake` and `cmake/DvvSpdx.cmake`; Task 6: bootstrap builds the validation layer (Python 3).
- Produces (tool): `cmake [-DMIN_VERSION=<major>.<minor>] [-DVULKANINFO=<program>] [-DSUMMARY_FILE=<file>] -P rawreel-workspace/tools/check-vulkan-device.cmake`: prints `vulkaninfo --summary`, then `-- Vulkan device check passed: apiVersion <versions> (<MIN_VERSION> required)`, or fails with `check-vulkan-device: vulkaninfo not found (Linux: install vulkan-tools)`, `check-vulkan-device: '<vulkaninfo> --summary' failed (<status>):`, `check-vulkan-device: vulkaninfo lists no Vulkan device`, `check-vulkan-device: no Vulkan device reports apiVersion <MIN_VERSION> or newer (found: <versions>)` or `check-vulkan-device: MIN_VERSION must be <major>.<minor>, got '<value>'`. Only `apiVersion` lines after `Devices:` count; both `1.4.318` and `4210688 (1.4.0)` are read; CRLF output is accepted.
- Produces (tests): CTest `workspace.check_vulkan_device` (the workspace build now has 6 workspace tests); tools-job step `Vulkan device check tests`.
- Produces (CI, `dvv-ci.yml`): step `Install and check the Vulkan device (Linux)` (`sudo apt-get install -y mesa-vulkan-drivers libvulkan1 vulkan-tools` and the device check); steps `Build and test (Windows)` (no variables: the GPU tests skip) and `Build and test (Linux)` (`DVV_REQUIRE_GPU: "1"`, `DVV_VK_VALIDATION: sync`, `DVV_VK_VALIDATION_REQUIRED: "1"`, all three Linux presets; `TSAN_OPTIONS` is never set); steps `Restore third-party` (id `third-party`, `actions/cache/restore@v5`, key `third-party-${{ runner.os }}-<toolchain>-${{ hashFiles('rawreel-workspace/deps/**', 'rawreel-workspace/cmake/DvvThirdParty.cmake', 'rawreel-workspace/cmake/DvvSpdx.cmake') }}`) before bootstrap and `Save third-party` (`actions/cache/save@v5`, only on a miss, key `steps.third-party.outputs.cache-primary-key`) right after it, in the build jobs and the lint job (the lint job uses the `linux-clang` entry, toolchain `clang`); lint job step `Build` (`cmake --preset ninja-clang` and `cmake --build --preset ninja-clang`) before `Tidy`. `workspace.yml`'s integration job gets the same Vulkan setup, split test step (`Build and test everything (Windows)` / `(Linux)`) and cache steps.

Note (publishing order): dvv-gpu's `ci.yml` calls `dvv-ci.yml@main`, so all three commits of this task must be on rawreel-workspace `main` before dvv-gpu's first CI run (Task 25). Without the Build step, clang-tidy in the lint job fails for dvv-gpu (`file not found` for a generated shader header; seen in the prototype's dry run of the old lint job), and without the Vulkan setup the Linux GPU tests skip instead of running. - [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

`rawreel-workspace/tests/ci/test_check_vulkan_device.cmake` (saved `vulkaninfo --summary` output in vulkaninfo's layout; the instance and layer versions above `Devices:` must never count; the last two cases run a vulkaninfo that fails: `cmake` itself, which rejects `--summary`, and a missing program):

```cmake
# Tests for tools/check-vulkan-device.cmake, mostly on saved `vulkaninfo --summary` output.
# Run: cmake -DWORK_DIR=<empty scratch dir> -P tests/ci/test_check_vulkan_device.cmake
cmake_minimum_required(VERSION 4.2)
if(NOT DEFINED WORK_DIR)
    message(FATAL_ERROR "pass -DWORK_DIR=<scratch directory>")
endif()
set(script "${CMAKE_CURRENT_LIST_DIR}/../../tools/check-vulkan-device.cmake")
file(REMOVE_RECURSE "${WORK_DIR}")
file(MAKE_DIRECTORY "${WORK_DIR}")

# check(<0|1 expected failure> <expected output regex> [-D<name>=<value>...])
function(check expect_failure expected_text)
    execute_process(COMMAND "${CMAKE_COMMAND}" ${ARGN} -P "${script}"
                    RESULT_VARIABLE status OUTPUT_VARIABLE out ERROR_VARIABLE err)
    string(REGEX REPLACE "[ \t\r\n]+" " " output "${out}${err}")
    if(expect_failure AND status EQUAL 0)
        message(FATAL_ERROR "FAIL: check-vulkan-device ${ARGN} succeeded:\n${out}${err}")
    elseif(NOT expect_failure AND NOT status EQUAL 0)
        message(FATAL_ERROR "FAIL: check-vulkan-device ${ARGN} failed:\n${out}${err}")
    elseif(NOT output MATCHES "${expected_text}")
        message(FATAL_ERROR "FAIL: output of check-vulkan-device ${ARGN} lacks '${expected_text}':\n"
                            "${out}${err}")
    endif()
endfunction()

# summary(<file name> <device block>...) writes a summary in vulkaninfo's layout: instance and layer
# versions first (never counted), then the devices.
function(summary name)
    string(CONCAT text "==========\nVULKANINFO\n==========\n\nVulkan Instance Version: 1.4.304\n\n"
                  "Instance Layers: count = 1\n--------------------------\n"
                  "VK_LAYER_MESA_device_select Linux device selection layer 1.4.303  version 1\n\n"
                  "Devices:\n========\n")
    set(index 0)
    foreach(block IN LISTS ARGN)
        string(APPEND text "GPU${index}:\n${block}")
        math(EXPR index "${index} + 1")
    endforeach()
    file(WRITE "${WORK_DIR}/${name}" "${text}")
endfunction()

string(CONCAT lavapipe "\tapiVersion         = 1.4.318\n\tdriverVersion      = 25.2.8\n"
              "\tdeviceType         = PHYSICAL_DEVICE_TYPE_CPU\n"
              "\tdeviceName         = llvmpipe (LLVM 20.1.2, 256 bits)\n")
string(CONCAT old_driver "\tapiVersion         = 1.3.289\n\tdriverVersion      = 24.2.8\n"
              "\tdeviceType         = PHYSICAL_DEVICE_TYPE_INTEGRATED_GPU\n")

summary(lavapipe.txt "${lavapipe}")
check(0 "Vulkan device check passed: apiVersion 1.4.318 \\(1.4 required\\)"
      "-DSUMMARY_FILE=${WORK_DIR}/lavapipe.txt")
summary(two.txt "${old_driver}" "${lavapipe}")
check(0 "passed: apiVersion 1.3.289, 1.4.318" "-DSUMMARY_FILE=${WORK_DIR}/two.txt")
# CRLF line ends (vulkaninfo on Windows).
file(READ "${WORK_DIR}/lavapipe.txt" text)
string(REPLACE "\n" "\r\n" text "${text}")
file(WRITE "${WORK_DIR}/crlf.txt" "${text}")
check(0 "passed: apiVersion 1.4.318" "-DSUMMARY_FILE=${WORK_DIR}/crlf.txt")
# Older vulkaninfo versions print the packed number first.
summary(packed.txt "\tapiVersion     = 4210688 (1.4.0)\n")
check(0 "passed: apiVersion 1.4.0" "-DSUMMARY_FILE=${WORK_DIR}/packed.txt")

summary(old.txt "${old_driver}")
check(1 "no Vulkan device reports apiVersion 1.4 or newer \\(found: 1.3.289\\)"
      "-DSUMMARY_FILE=${WORK_DIR}/old.txt")
check(1 "no Vulkan device reports apiVersion 1.5 or newer \\(found: 1.4.318\\)"
      "-DSUMMARY_FILE=${WORK_DIR}/lavapipe.txt" -DMIN_VERSION=1.5)
check(0 "passed: apiVersion 1.3.289 \\(1.3 required\\)"
      "-DSUMMARY_FILE=${WORK_DIR}/old.txt" -DMIN_VERSION=1.3)
summary(none.txt)
check(1 "vulkaninfo lists no Vulkan device" "-DSUMMARY_FILE=${WORK_DIR}/none.txt")
# The instance version above "Devices:" is 1.4.304 but does not count.
file(WRITE "${WORK_DIR}/no-devices.txt"
     "Vulkan Instance Version: 1.4.304\n\nInstance Layers: count = 0\n")
check(1 "vulkaninfo lists no Vulkan device" "-DSUMMARY_FILE=${WORK_DIR}/no-devices.txt")
check(1 "MIN_VERSION must be <major>.<minor>, got '1.4.0'"
      "-DSUMMARY_FILE=${WORK_DIR}/lavapipe.txt" -DMIN_VERSION=1.4.0)

# A failing vulkaninfo fails the check: cmake itself rejects --summary.
check(1 "--summary' failed" "-DVULKANINFO=${CMAKE_COMMAND}")
check(1 "--summary' failed" "-DVULKANINFO=${WORK_DIR}/no-such-program")

message(STATUS "check-vulkan-device tests passed")
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement the device check**

`rawreel-workspace/tools/check-vulkan-device.cmake`:

```cmake
# Checks that a Vulkan device for the GPU tests is present (spec §13.4): prints the output of
# `vulkaninfo --summary` and fails unless at least one device reports an apiVersion of MIN_VERSION
# or newer. CI runs it on Linux before the build, whose GPU tests then must not skip
# (DVV_REQUIRE_GPU=1).
#
# Usage: cmake [-DMIN_VERSION=<major>.<minor>] [-DVULKANINFO=<program>]
#              -P tools/check-vulkan-device.cmake
#   MIN_VERSION  lowest accepted device API version (default 1.4)
#   VULKANINFO   the vulkaninfo program (default: vulkaninfo from PATH; Linux package vulkan-tools)
#   SUMMARY_FILE checks saved `vulkaninfo --summary` output instead of running vulkaninfo (tests)
cmake_minimum_required(VERSION 4.2)

if(NOT DEFINED MIN_VERSION)
    set(MIN_VERSION 1.4)
endif()
if(NOT MIN_VERSION MATCHES "^[0-9]+\\.[0-9]+$")
    message(FATAL_ERROR
            "check-vulkan-device: MIN_VERSION must be <major>.<minor>, got '${MIN_VERSION}'")
endif()

if(DEFINED SUMMARY_FILE)
    file(READ "${SUMMARY_FILE}" summary)
else()
    if(NOT DEFINED VULKANINFO)
        find_program(VULKANINFO vulkaninfo)
        if(NOT VULKANINFO)
            message(FATAL_ERROR
                    "check-vulkan-device: vulkaninfo not found (Linux: install vulkan-tools)")
        endif()
    endif()
    execute_process(COMMAND "${VULKANINFO}" --summary
                    RESULT_VARIABLE status OUTPUT_VARIABLE summary ERROR_VARIABLE errors)
    message("${summary}")
    if(NOT status EQUAL 0)
        message(FATAL_ERROR "check-vulkan-device: '${VULKANINFO} --summary' failed (${status}):\n"
                            "${errors}")
    endif()
endif()

# Each device block lists "apiVersion = 1.4.318"; older vulkaninfo versions print
# "apiVersion = 4206847 (1.3.255)". The instance and layer versions above "Devices:" do not count.
string(REPLACE "\r\n" "\n" summary "${summary}")
string(FIND "${summary}" "\nDevices:" devices_at)
set(versions "")
set(found FALSE)
if(NOT devices_at EQUAL -1)
    string(SUBSTRING "${summary}" ${devices_at} -1 devices)
    string(REGEX MATCHALL "apiVersion[ \t]*=[^\n]*" lines "${devices}")
    foreach(line IN LISTS lines)
        if(line MATCHES "([0-9]+)\\.([0-9]+)\\.([0-9]+)")
            set(version "${CMAKE_MATCH_1}.${CMAKE_MATCH_2}.${CMAKE_MATCH_3}")
            list(APPEND versions ${version})
            if(version VERSION_GREATER_EQUAL MIN_VERSION)
                set(found TRUE)
            endif()
        endif()
    endforeach()
endif()

if(NOT versions)
    message(FATAL_ERROR "check-vulkan-device: vulkaninfo lists no Vulkan device")
endif()
list(JOIN versions ", " version_list)
if(NOT found)
    message(FATAL_ERROR "check-vulkan-device: no Vulkan device reports apiVersion ${MIN_VERSION} or "
                        "newer (found: ${version_list})")
endif()
message(STATUS "Vulkan device check passed: apiVersion ${version_list} (${MIN_VERSION} required)")
```

wsl -d Ubuntu-24.04 -e bash -lc '. _build/tools/linux/env.sh && cmake "-DWORK_DIR=$PWD/build-scratch-linux" -P rawreel-workspace/tests/ci/test_check_vulkan_device.cmake'
cmake -P rawreel-workspace/tools/check-vulkan-device.cmake 2>&1 | Select-String 'check'
wsl -d Ubuntu-24.04 -e bash -lc '. _build/tools/linux/env.sh && apt-get -s install -y mesa-vulkan-drivers libvulkan1 vulkan-tools | tail -n 1 && env -u DISPLAY -u WAYLAND_DISPLAY cmake -P rawreel-workspace/tools/check-vulkan-device.cmake 2>&1 | grep -E "deviceName|apiVersion|check"; echo exit ${PIPESTATUS[0]}'
wsl -d Ubuntu-24.04 -e bash -lc '. _build/tools/linux/env.sh && VK_DRIVER_FILES=/nonexistent.json cmake -P rawreel-workspace/tools/check-vulkan-device.cmake 2>&1 | grep -E "check-vulkan-device|ERROR_INCOMPATIBLE_DRIVER"; echo exit ${PIPESTATUS[0]}'
wsl -d Ubuntu-24.04 -e bash -lc '. _build/tools/linux/env.sh && cmake -DMIN_VERSION=1.5 -P rawreel-workspace/tools/check-vulkan-device.cmake 2>&1 | grep -A1 "check-vulkan-device:"; echo exit ${PIPESTATUS[0]}'
Remove-Item -Recurse -Force build-scratch, build-scratch-linux
```

Run each line in its own tool call. Expected:
- Both test runs print `-- check-vulkan-device tests passed` (exit 0). The WSL run needs an absolute `WORK_DIR` (`$PWD/...`, as CI's `runner.temp`).
- Windows (the device check runs the `vulkaninfo` that the GPU driver installs): `-- Vulkan device check passed: apiVersion <the GPU's version> (1.4 required)`. On a machine without `vulkaninfo` the line reads `check-vulkan-device: vulkaninfo not found (Linux: install vulkan-tools)` instead; write that down and go on (CI runs the check on Linux only).
- WSL, the CI step's commands (apt simulated, no sudo; `DISPLAY` unset as on a runner): `0 upgraded, 0 newly installed, ...` (Task 0 installed the packages), then lavapipe's `apiVersion = 1.4.318` and `deviceName = llvmpipe (...)` (another WSL device may be listed too), `-- Vulkan device check passed: apiVersion 1.4.318 (1.4 required)` (or `..., 1.4.318` after another device's version) and `exit 0`.
- Forced failures: with no driver, in this order, a `CMake Error at .../check-vulkan-device.cmake:<line> (message):` line (the grep matches the script's path), `check-vulkan-device: '/usr/bin/vulkaninfo --summary' failed (1):`, a line with vulkaninfo's error `ERROR_INCOMPATIBLE_DRIVER` (part of the message) and `exit 1`; with `MIN_VERSION=1.5`, `check-vulkan-device: no Vulkan device reports apiVersion 1.5 or newer` / `(found: 1.4.318)` and `exit 1`.

the build instead of failing the GPU tests later. Only the device blocks count, not the instance or
layer versions; both vulkaninfo formats (1.4.318 and "4210688 (1.4.0)") are read. The tests check
saved summaries and a failing vulkaninfo; they run in the workspace build and the tools job.

- [ ] **Step 10: Cache third-party in every job and build before clang-tidy**

A cold third-party build now takes 15 minutes or more (the validation layer; 940 s in the prototype's WSL dry run). The plain `actions/cache` step saved the prefix only at the end of a successful job, so a failing test threw the build away; restore and save are now separate steps around bootstrap (`actions/cache/restore@v5` and `actions/cache/save@v5`, the current major like `checkout@v5`; the inputs and outputs used here are the same as in v4). The key is unchanged and hashes the inputs of bootstrap's own stamp, so a restored prefix is "up to date" and not rebuilt.

In `rawreel-workspace/.github/workflows/dvv-ci.yml` replace

```yaml
          Write-Host "Visual Studio $version"
      - uses: actions/cache@v4
        with:
```

with

```yaml
          Write-Host "Visual Studio $version"
      # The key hashes the inputs of bootstrap's own up-to-date stamp, so a restored prefix is not
      # rebuilt. A miss builds all of it; the validation layer takes most of that time.
      - name: Restore third-party
        id: third-party
        uses: actions/cache/restore@v5
        with:
```

In `rawreel-workspace/.github/workflows/dvv-ci.yml` replace

```yaml
        run: ./rawreel-workspace/bootstrap.sh --toolchain ${{ matrix.toolchain }}
      # Windows runners have no GPU: the GPU tests exit with 77 and CTest reports them as skipped.
```

with

```yaml
        run: ./rawreel-workspace/bootstrap.sh --toolchain ${{ matrix.toolchain }}
      # Saved right after bootstrap, so a failing build or test does not throw the third-party build
      # away. When parallel jobs miss together, the first save wins and the others only warn.
      - name: Save third-party
        if: steps.third-party.outputs.cache-hit != 'true'
        uses: actions/cache/save@v5
        with:
          path: _third_party/${{ matrix.toolchain }}
          key: ${{ steps.third-party.outputs.cache-primary-key }}
      # Windows runners have no GPU: the GPU tests exit with 77 and CTest reports them as skipped.
```

In `rawreel-workspace/.github/workflows/dvv-ci.yml` replace

```yaml

    timeout-minutes: 30
    steps:
```

with

```yaml

    timeout-minutes: 60
    steps:
```

with

        id: third-party
        uses: actions/cache/restore@v5
        with:
          path: _third_party/clang
          key: third-party-${{ runner.os }}-clang-${{ hashFiles('rawreel-workspace/deps/**', 'rawreel-workspace/cmake/DvvThirdParty.cmake', 'rawreel-workspace/cmake/DvvSpdx.cmake') }}
      - name: Bootstrap
        run: ./rawreel-workspace/bootstrap.sh --toolchain clang
      - name: Save third-party
        if: steps.third-party.outputs.cache-hit != 'true'
        uses: actions/cache/save@v5
        with:

          key: ${{ steps.third-party.outputs.cache-primary-key }}
      # clang-tidy reads sources that include generated files (e.g. dvv_add_shaders headers), which
      # exist only after a build.

        working-directory: ${{ inputs.repo }}
        run: |
          cmake --preset ninja-clang
          cmake --build --preset ninja-clang
      - name: Tidy
        working-directory: ${{ inputs.repo }}
        run: |
          # :(glob) stops * at '/', so tests/compile_fail and tests/package (built separately) stay out.
```

@'
ci: cache third-party in every job right after bootstrap and build before clang-tidy

With the validation layer, an uncached third-party build takes most of a job's time. The build jobs,
the lint job and the nightly integration job now restore the prefix before bootstrap and, on a miss,
save it right after bootstrap (actions/cache/restore and save), so a failing build or test no longer
throws the third-party build away. The lint job uses the same entry as the linux-clang build jobs;
its timeout is 60 minutes for a cold cache. clang-tidy reads tests that include headers generated at
build time (dvv_add_shaders), so the lint job builds the ninja-clang Debug tree before running it.

```

**Verified only on GitHub** (after publishing, Task 25; record the results in the progress ledger):
- The `ubuntu-24.04` runners get Mesa ≥ 25 lavapipe from `noble-updates` (`apt-get install` upgrades a preinstalled older `mesa-vulkan-drivers`) and `vulkaninfo --summary` runs headless there: the step `Install and check the Vulkan device (Linux)` prints `Vulkan device check passed: apiVersion 1.4.x`.
- The Linux GPU tests run (not skipped) with sync validation in `linux-clang`, `linux-clang-asan` and `linux-clang-tsan`; LeakSanitizer and TSan with Mesa on the runner's kernel (clean in WSL).
- The nightly integration job's Linux leg on the runner: the workspace build runs the GPU tests (not skipped) with the three variables (run in WSL in Step 5, never on a runner).
- The Windows runners have no GPU and the GPU tests report as skipped (exit 77), whether the image has a Vulkan loader or not (no loader: `Unsupported`; a loader without a driver: `ERROR_INCOMPATIBLE_DRIVER` → `Unsupported`); this was only simulated locally.
- Cold-cache times on the runners (expected 15–25 minutes for third-party per toolchain) within the 60-minute build and lint timeouts and the 90-minute nightly job; the second run restores the cache (`Restore third-party` hits, `Save third-party` is skipped, bootstrap reports up to date).
- `actions/cache/save` from parallel jobs with the same key: the first save wins and the others only warn, never fail; the cache entries (about 0.5 GB for `msvc`) stay within the 10 GB per-repository limit.
- The lint job: Build and Tidy for dvv-gpu within 60 minutes (clang-tidy runs the files one after another; if that gets tight, `xargs -0 -r -P "$(nproc)"` is the cheap fix, not done because it is unverified on a runner).

```markdown
  `when_all`, executors and `stop_token` integration — no generators or custom allocators in v1.
- The record function passed to `submit` runs on the submit thread and only records commands: it
  must not block or wait for another submission, since every later submission of the device waits
  behind it.
- Queue access from the submit and presenter threads is serialised by a per-`VkQueue` mutex.
```

```markdown
### 5.1 Vulkan baseline
- **Vulkan 1.4** instance and device. Required features are checked at startup and listed on the
  Capabilities page: timeline semaphores, synchronization2, dynamic rendering, buffer device address,
  descriptor indexing, 16-bit storage, maintenance5/6. `shaderInt16`/`shaderFloat16` are optional (shader
  variants without them exist); `hostImageCopy` and `VK_EXT_memory_budget` are used when present.
- Missing loader or a device below 1.4 → a clear startup dialog (GUI) or exit code 4 (CLI).
- Debug builds enable the validation layer (and synchronization validation in tests) when installed.

### 5.2 Devices
- One `VkInstance`; `GpuRegistry` enumerates GPUs, records capabilities and ranks them.
- One `VkDevice` per used GPU, wrapped in an **`ExecutionContext`**: queues, VMA allocator, on-disk pipeline cache (per-user cache dir), bindless descriptor heap,
  `ResourcePool`, submitter. Components reach the GPU **only** through an `ExecutionContext`.
- v1: one active GPU, selectable in Preferences. v1.x: placement of streams/work across GPUs.
```

with

```markdown
### 5.1 Vulkan baseline
- **Vulkan 1.4** devices. The instance requests API 1.4 but accepts a loader ≥ 1.3 (Ubuntu 24.04 ships
  loader 1.3.275, which drives a 1.4 device): vk-bootstrap `set_minimum_instance_version(1, 3, 0)` and
  `require_api_version(1, 4, 0)`. A device below 1.4 is listed but not usable. Required features are
  checked at startup and listed on the Capabilities page: timeline semaphores, synchronization2, dynamic
  rendering, buffer device address, descriptor indexing, 16-bit storage, maintenance5/6. `shaderInt16`/
  `shaderFloat16` are optional (shader variants without them exist); `hostImageCopy` and
  `VK_EXT_memory_budget` are used when present.
- Missing loader or a device below 1.4 → a clear startup dialog (GUI) or exit code 4 (CLI).
- **Validation** modes `off`, `on`, `sync` (synchronization validation), from the options or the
  environment variable `DVV_VK_VALIDATION`; the default is `on` in Debug builds when the layer is present.
  Only `VK_LAYER_KHRONOS_validation` ≥ 1.4 counts: an older one (e.g. a system's 1.3) does not know
  Vulkan 1.4, reports false errors and is treated as absent. The tests load the layer built from source
  in the third-party prefix (§13.2). `DVV_VK_VALIDATION_REQUIRED=1` turns a missing layer into an error
  (CI). The messenger routes every message to the dvv logger and counts errors of any type and warnings
  of the validation and performance types; tests fail on a count above zero.
- Synchronization validation cannot see buffer-device-address accesses, so the command recorder inserts
  barriers itself (§16).

### 5.2 Devices
- One `VkInstance`; `GpuRegistry` enumerates GPUs, records capabilities and ranks them (usable first;
  discrete > integrated > virtual > CPU; then device-local memory). It reports them as `GpuInfo`;
  dvv-gpu does not depend on dvv-registry, so a higher layer turns `GpuInfo` into registry
  `Capabilities`. `dvv-gpu-info` prints them (text or `--json`; exit 4 without a usable device).
- One `VkDevice` per used GPU, wrapped in an **`ExecutionContext`**: queues, VMA allocator, on-disk pipeline cache (per-user cache dir, §5.4), bindless descriptor heap,
  `ResourcePool`s, submitter. Components reach the GPU **only** through an `ExecutionContext`.
- v1: one active GPU, selectable in Preferences. v1.x: placement of streams/work across GPUs.
```

In `rawreel-workspace/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-19-rawreel-design.md` (under "### 5.3 Resources") replace

```markdown
- `ResourcePool`: transient buffers/images reused by `(size, format, usage)`, returned after retire.
- Non-frame GPU resources: swapchain images (`Presenter`, one per viewport), composite/overlay targets,
```

with

```markdown
- `ResourcePool`: transient buffers/images reused by `(size, format, usage)`, returned after retire.
  M1a pools buffers only, keyed by `(size, usage, memory)`; images follow in M2 (§15). A pool is created
  by `ExecutionContext::createResourcePool()` and keeps its context alive. `acquireBuffer()` hands out a
  lease (a `shared_ptr` whose release returns the buffer to the pool); each buffer is created and
  registered once (§9.4). A returned buffer is tagged with the latest timeline value reserved at its
  return and handed out again only after the GPU has finished that submission, so it is never reused
  while a submission made or being recorded at its return may still use it. Shaders reach buffers by
  device address, so it is the lease, not the command recorder, that keeps a pooled buffer from being
  reused: users keep the lease alive (e.g. captured in the record function) until the submission that
  uses it has completed.
- Non-frame GPU resources: swapchain images (`Presenter`, one per viewport), composite/overlay targets,
```

In `rawreel-workspace/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-19-rawreel-design.md` (under "### 5.4 Shaders and pipelines") replace

```markdown
- Built-in shaders are Slang modules compiled by `slangc` at build time to SPIR-V, embedded as generated
  C++ arrays; the reflection JSON is turned into `ParameterSchema` code.
- User shaders are compiled at run time by the dynamically loaded Slang library; reflection generates the
```

with

```markdown
- Built-in shaders are Slang modules compiled by `slangc` at build time to SPIR-V, embedded as generated
  C++ arrays; the reflection JSON is turned into `ParameterSchema` code. M1a embeds the reflection JSON
  next to the SPIR-V (`dvv::gpu::EmbeddedShader`); the conversion to a schema arrives with the first
  component that needs it (§15), because dvv-gpu does not depend on dvv-registry. Until then the C++
  side declares a shader's push-constant struct by hand.
- `dvv_add_shaders(<target> [NAMESPACE <ns>] SHADERS <file.slang>…)` (dvv-gpu, §13.2) generates one
  header per shader, `<ns as a path>/<name>.h`, declaring `const dvv::gpu::EmbeddedShader&
  <ns>::<name in camelCase>() noexcept`. The namespace defaults to `<target>_shaders`, so the headers of
  two libraries never collide. Each shader has one compute entry point `main`; two shaders of a target
  that give the same function name, or a name that is a C++ keyword, fail configuring. The target must
  use the keyword form of `target_link_libraries()` (the function links `dvv::gpu` `PUBLIC`), and the
  generated headers exist in the build tree only; they are not installed with the target.
- Compute pipelines (`ExecutionContext::createComputePipeline(shader, push-constant size)`, managed)
  take their SPIR-V chained into the pipeline create info (maintenance5, no `VkShaderModule`), have one
  compute push-constant range at offset 0 and, in M1a, no descriptor sets (buffers by device address
  only). The push-constant size must be a multiple of 4 and cover the shader's push-constant block in
  its reflection JSON; a size above the device's `maxPushConstantsSize` is `Unsupported` (§11).
  `CommandRecorder::dispatch` takes exactly that many push-constant bytes and group counts within the
  device's limits. A pipeline and its layout retire like resources (§5.3).
- **Pipeline cache:** one `VkPipelineCache` per context, kept in the file `ContextOptions` names (empty:
  in memory only). The default, `defaultPipelineCacheFile(app, device)`, is one file per device,
  `vulkan-pipelines-<device UUID>.bin` in the per-user cache directory, since a cache only fits the
  device and driver build that wrote it. The file holds the driver's data followed by a dvv trailer
  (data size, checksum, magic). Before the data reaches the driver, the header is validated (header size
  exactly 32, header version one, vendor id, device id, pipeline-cache UUID) and the trailer checked; a
  corrupt, truncated or foreign file is ignored with a log line, and data the driver still rejects is
  replaced by an empty cache. Saving is atomic: each save writes a temporary file of its own and renames
  it over the cache file (concurrent savers leave one complete file, last writer wins). The cache is
  saved by `savePipelineCache()` and when the context closes, after its threads have stopped; on
  close only when its data changed since it was loaded or last saved, or the file is gone.
- User shaders are compiled at run time by the dynamically loaded Slang library; reflection generates the
```

- `probe` runs at startup and when the GPU changes; failure greys the component out with the reason.

### 9.3 Component kinds

with

````markdown
struct ComponentInfo { std::string_view id, name; Kind kind; int version; };
template <class T, class I>                 // used as Component<IMetric> T
concept Component = std::derived_from<T, I> && requires(const Capabilities& caps) {
    { T::info() } noexcept -> std::same_as<ComponentInfo>;
    { T::schema() } -> std::same_as<const ParameterSchema&>;
    { T::probe(caps) } -> std::same_as<std::expected<void, Unavailable>>;
};
```

- `info()` is `noexcept`: the registry needs the id to report anything about a component. A `schema()`
  or `probe()` that throws, and a constructor that throws (except `std::bad_alloc`, which is
  `OutOfMemory` and leaves the component available), make the component unavailable with an
  `Internal` reason; the rest of the registry keeps working.
- `probe` runs at startup and when the GPU changes; failure greys the component out with the reason.
- `ParameterSchema`: int, float, bool, enum, range, colour with min/max/default → wxPropertyGrid, CLI
  `--param name=value`, session file. User shader schemas come from Slang reflection. A schema is built
  with `ParameterSchema::Builder`, whose `build()` returns `Errc::Internal` for a broken declaration
  (duplicate or invalid name, bad limits, a default that does not fit); values that do not fit the schema
  are `Errc::Format`.

### 9.3 Component kinds
````

In `rawreel-workspace/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-19-rawreel-design.md` (under "### 9.4 Object creation and lifetime (global rule)") replace

```markdown
**Scope.**
- *Managed objects:* every component (§9.3) and every service or resource in `dvv::` — `ExecutionContext`,
  `GpuBuffer`/`GpuImage`, `ResourcePool`, `Presenter`, `FrameStore`, `IFrame` implementations, `ISource`
  instances.
- *Handles* (exempt; move-only RAII, created only by their owner but not registered individually):
  leases, `FrameRef`, `Task`/coroutine frames, awaiters. The `FrameStore` accounts for leases (§6.5).
- *Value types* (exempt): `FrameKey`, `ViewSpec`, `FrameDesc`, `Error`, format descriptors, result values,
```

with

```markdown
**Scope.**
- *Managed objects:* every component (§9.3) and every service or resource in `dvv::` —
  `ComponentRegistry`, `GpuRegistry`, `ExecutionContext`, `GpuBuffer`/`GpuImage`, `ResourcePool`,
  `ComputePipeline`, `Presenter`, `FrameStore`, `IFrame` implementations, `ISource` instances.
  `ComponentRegistry` and `GpuRegistry` are their own creation authorities (a static `create()`); the
  `GpuRegistry` creates `ExecutionContext`s, and an `ExecutionContext` creates its buffers, images,
  `ResourcePool`s and `ComputePipeline`s.
- *Internal parts* (exempt): implementation objects owned only by managed objects and created only by
  them, e.g. dvv-gpu's `InstanceCore` (the instance and messenger of a `GpuRegistry`), `DeviceCore` (the
  device, queue, allocator and retire queue, shared by an `ExecutionContext` and its resources until
  their deferred destruction is done), `Submitter` (the context's submit and waiter threads) and
  `PipelineCache` (the context's pipeline cache and its file). They are never handed out and are not
  registered; the managed objects that hold them are, so leaks still show.
- *Handles* (exempt; move-only RAII, created only by their owner but not registered individually):
  leases, `FrameRef`, `Task`/coroutine frames, awaiters, and the `CommandRecorder` passed to a record
  function (valid only during that call). The `FrameStore` accounts for leases (§6.5).
- *Value types* (exempt): `FrameKey`, `ViewSpec`, `FrameDesc`, `Error`, format descriptors, result values,
```

  the error early. GNU statement-expressions (`({ … })`) are not used (MSVC and `-Wpedantic`).

- Exceptions stay enabled (wx, std) but never cross a component boundary: the registry wrapper converts
```

with

```markdown
  the error early. GNU statement-expressions (`({ … })`) are not used (MSVC and `-Wpedantic`).
- Vulkan-Hpp with `VULKAN_HPP_NO_EXCEPTIONS`; results converted to `Error` in dvv-gpu only, with the
  message `<call> failed: <result name> (VkResult <n>)`:

  | `VkResult` | `Errc` |
  |---|---|
  | `ERROR_DEVICE_LOST` | `DeviceLost` |
  | `ERROR_OUT_OF_HOST_MEMORY`, `ERROR_OUT_OF_DEVICE_MEMORY`, `ERROR_OUT_OF_POOL_MEMORY`, `ERROR_FRAGMENTED_POOL`, `ERROR_FRAGMENTATION`, `ERROR_MEMORY_MAP_FAILED`, `ERROR_TOO_MANY_OBJECTS` | `OutOfMemory` |
  | `ERROR_INITIALIZATION_FAILED`, `ERROR_INCOMPATIBLE_DRIVER`, `ERROR_LAYER_NOT_PRESENT`, `ERROR_EXTENSION_NOT_PRESENT`, `ERROR_FEATURE_NOT_PRESENT`, `ERROR_FORMAT_NOT_SUPPORTED`, `ERROR_NOT_PERMITTED`, `ERROR_IMAGE_USAGE_NOT_SUPPORTED_KHR` | `Unsupported` |
  | any other error | `Internal` |

  A missing Vulkan loader is `Unsupported` ("Vulkan loader not found"); programming errors such as an
  unknown `DeviceId` or a zero-sized buffer are `Internal`. Loaders report a listed validation layer
  whose library does not load as `ERROR_LAYER_NOT_PRESENT` (Linux loader 1.3.275) or
  `ERROR_OUT_OF_HOST_MEMORY` (Windows loader 1.4.341): unless validation is required, the instance is
  built once more without the layer (a real out-of-memory then fails again), and the loader's messages
  from the failed attempt do not count as validation findings. With validation required the error
  stays as the loader reported it (`Unsupported` on Linux, `OutOfMemory` on Windows).

  GPU work: a command the recorder cannot record (a resource or pipeline of another device or
  generation, a range outside a buffer, a missing usage flag, a push-constant size other than the
  pipeline's, group counts outside the device's limits, an unsupported format or layout) is a
  programming error, `Internal`: nothing more is recorded, and the submission completes with that error
  after signalling its timeline value. `createComputePipeline` returns `Internal` for data that is not
  SPIR-V and for a push-constant size that is not a multiple of 4 or smaller than the shader's block,
  `Format` for reflection JSON it cannot read, and `Unsupported` for a size above the device's limit.
  A pipeline-cache file that cannot be written is `Io` from `savePipelineCache()` and a log line when
  the context closes; a cache file that cannot be used is never an error (§5.4).
- Exceptions stay enabled (wx, std) but never cross a component boundary: the registry wrapper converts
```

suppression file that matches only reports with a frame inside the driver (`race:libvulkan_lvp.so`);

with

```markdown
The first plan covers **M0**.

**M1a demo** (dvv-gpu GPU tests, §12): `tests/gpu_test_compute.cpp` runs the `slangc`-built `add_one`
shader over a buffer (round trip of 1000 values), two dependent dispatches in one submission (the
barrier check of §16) and a pooled buffer reused only after the GPU finished with it;
`gpu_test_submission.cpp` holds the buffer → image → buffer round trip and the deferred destruction of
resources a submission uses, `gpu_test_pipeline.cpp` the pipeline-cache round trip. `dvv-gpu-info`
prints the capabilities data.

**Deferred from M1a** (each lands with the milestone or component that first needs it):
- `dvv_add_component()` with `KIND`, `SHADERS`, `REQUIRES` and the per-component CMake option (§9.1), and
  the component conformance test (§12): with dvv-components.
- Component interfaces (`ISource`, `IMetric`, …) and the check that `ComponentInfo::kind` matches the
  registered interface: with dvv-media and dvv-components. Version migration of session parameters:
  with the Session (M3).
- Slang reflection → `ParameterSchema` (§5.4), which also replaces the push-constant structs written by
  hand (today only their size is checked against the reflection): with the first component that has
  shader parameters.
- A test that editing a Slang module a shader imports rebuilds the shader (`dvv_add_shaders` passes
  slangc's depfile to the build, untested so far): with the first shader that imports a module.
- Images in `ResourcePool` (§5.3): M2. A leased image needs another design, because the command
  recorder keeps images through `shared_from_this()`, which a lease must not hand out.
- Images in shaders and descriptor sets (bindless heap, §5.4): M2.
- Tests of the out-of-memory paths (the submitter's signal-only batch and host-signal fallback, the
  resource pool's give-back) and of the pipeline cache's rarer paths (data the driver rejects after
  validation, a save that cannot write): reviewed, not tested; with fault injection (§12).
- Finer, per-resource barriers instead of the recorder's global barrier (§16): once measured.
- Automatic `flush()`/`invalidate()` of host-visible buffers around submissions (today the caller
  calls them; both do nothing on host-coherent memory): once a component needs it.
- TSan suppressions built into the test executable (`__tsan_default_suppressions()`) instead of
  `TSAN_OPTIONS` (§12): optional.
- The Linux validation layer is built without window-system support (headless M1a; `vulkaninfo` with
  the layer reports false surface errors). M1b builds it with XCB/Xlib/Wayland, which needs their
  development packages on Linux.
- Loading `vulkan-1.dll` from the system directory only (today: the default DLL search order): with
  dvv-platform's dynamic-library loading (M1b).
- vk-bootstrap 1.4.365 passes empty application and engine names to the driver (upstream bug): fixed by
  a later vk-bootstrap or a post-install patch.

---
```

  them (e.g. `/PDBALTPATH:%_PDB%`, build in a neutral root) before release artifacts are published.

with

```markdown
  them (e.g. `/PDBALTPATH:%_PDB%`, build in a neutral root) before release artifacts are published.
- **Synchronization validation does not see buffer device addresses** (M1a spike): a missing barrier
  between two dispatches that use the same buffer through its address goes unreported. The command
  recorder therefore inserts barriers automatically and conservatively: a global memory barrier (all
  earlier compute and transfer writes → later reads and writes) before every work command (dispatch,
  copy, fill, image transition, the latter combined with the layout change), and at the end of every
  submission a barrier that makes all writes visible to the host. Measured in M1a: the dependent-dispatch
  test (two `add_one` dispatches over the same 1000 values in one submission, §15) fails without the
  automatic barrier on NVIDIA hardware in 5 of 5 runs, every value incremented only once, while
  synchronization validation (required and active) reports nothing; with the barrier it passes. Commands
  recorded through `raw()` get the barrier before them but must order themselves among each other (and
  bring their own barriers for stages other than compute and transfer). Finer, per-resource barriers
  are an optimisation for later, once measured.
- **Slang licence:** checked at pinning (M1a). The conservative SPDX expression of the release archive
  includes `GPL-3.0-or-later WITH Bison-exception-2.2`, `NCSA` and `CC-BY-4.0`; it passes the gate only
  as a build tool (§13.2, §17). Loading `libslang` at run time for user shaders (v1.x) needs a new
  licence review.
- **wx 3.3 native child window + direct Vulkan surface on GTK/X11:** proven with SDL in the predecessor
```

In `rawreel-workspace/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-19-rawreel-design.md` (under "## 17. Review decisions") replace

```markdown
  `std::generator`, and `std::jthread` needs `-fexperimental-library` there.
```

with

```markdown
  `std::generator`, and `std::jthread` needs `-fexperimental-library` there.

Decisions taken during M1a (2026-10-20 and 2026-10-21):
- **Device API 1.4, loader ≥ 1.3** (§5.1): the instance requests 1.4 but accepts Ubuntu 24.04's 1.3.275
  loader. The validation layer must be ≥ 1.4 and comes from the third-party prefix (§5.1, §13.2).
- **Tool-only licences** (user decision, 2026-10-20): `NCSA` and `CC-BY-4.0` are accepted only for
  `tool` linkage, never for static or run-time libraries (§13.2). Slang's `slangc` needs them.
- **Third-party sources compiled into a dvv target never fail the build on warnings** (user decision,
  2026-10-21): per-source `/WX-` (MSVC) or `-Wno-error` (Clang) cancels the target's warnings as errors
  for that file only; the warnings stay visible (§13.3). First case: `VkBootstrap.cpp` in dvv-gpu.
- **Third-party headers are always `SYSTEM`** (user decision, 2026-10-21): the clang-tidy
  `HeaderFilterRegex` `.*/(include/dvv|src)/.*` stays as it is (it must cover private headers in `src/`),
  although it also matches third-party paths containing `/src/` (such as the prefix's
  `src/vk-bootstrap`); `SYSTEM` include directories keep those headers out of the checks (§13.3).
- **GPU tests** (§12): a separate executable per repository, label `gpu`, exit 77 = skipped unless
  `DVV_REQUIRE_GPU=1`; every test fails on a counted validation message; the sanitizer jobs run them too.
- **TSan and uninstrumented drivers** (§12): TSan builds suppress only reports with a frame inside the
  Vulkan driver (`tests/tsan.supp`, `race:libvulkan_lvp.so`, passed by CTest through `TSAN_OPTIONS` in
  thread-sanitizer builds); races in dvv code are still reported.
- **Automatic conservative barriers** in the command recorder, because synchronization validation
  cannot see buffer device addresses (§16); without them the dependent-dispatch test fails on hardware
  while validation stays silent.
- **ResourcePool returns a buffer at once, tagged** (§5.3): instead of "returned once retired", a
  released lease goes back to the pool immediately with the latest reserved timeline value and is handed
  out again only once that value is reached (the same guarantee, no extra queue). Images wait for M2.
- **Pipeline cache** (§5.4): one file per device by default; header validated and a dvv checksum
  trailer checked before the driver sees the data; an unusable file is ignored with a log line, never an
  error; saved atomically on request and, when it changed, on close. `PipelineCache`, like
  `Submitter`, is an internal part, not a managed object (§9.4).
- **Errors of GPU work** (§11): commands the recorder cannot record and malformed pipeline requests are
  programming errors (`Internal`); a push-constant size above the device's limit is `Unsupported`.
- **Layering:** dvv-gpu depends only on dvv-base in M1a (dvv-platform arrives in M1b); GPU capabilities
  are `GpuInfo`, turned into registry `Capabilities` by a higher layer (§5.2).
- **vk-bootstrap builds only the instance**; physical devices, the feature check and the device use
  dvv-gpu's own Vulkan-Hpp code with explicit dispatchers (one per instance and per device, no global
  default dispatcher). The loader is loaded at run time and never unloaded, because vk-bootstrap keeps
  the first `vkGetInstanceProcAddr`.
```

with

````markdown
`bootstrap` clones missing sibling repositories, sets their git hooks and commit identity and builds
the third-party dependencies (the first run builds the Vulkan validation layer from source, 10 to 20
minutes depending on the machine; later runs skip the build while `deps/` and the superbuild modules
are unchanged). The workspace build compiles every repository from its sibling folder as one
solution; each repository can also be built alone with the same preset names.

## GPU tests

The GPU tests (CTest label `gpu`, in dvv-gpu; the compute demo is `tests/gpu_test_compute.cpp`) need
a Vulkan 1.4 device. Without one they exit with 77 and CTest reports them as skipped, so a machine
without a GPU still passes. CTest points the Vulkan loader at the validation layer that bootstrap
built in `_third_party/<toolchain>`; a system layer older than 1.4 is ignored. Every GPU test fails
on a validation message. Settings, read from the environment:

| Variable | Values | Effect |
|---|---|---|
| `DVV_REQUIRE_GPU` | `1` | a missing device fails the GPU tests instead of skipping them |
| `DVV_VK_VALIDATION` | `off`, `on`, `sync` | validation mode; default `on` in Debug when the layer is present, else `off` |
| `DVV_VK_VALIDATION_REQUIRED` | `0`, `1` | `1`: a missing validation layer is an error; with `0` validation is just switched off |

CI's Linux jobs run them on lavapipe with all three set (`1`, `sync`, `1`); Windows CI has no GPU.
Locally, Windows (PowerShell 7), from a repository folder:

```powershell
$env:DVV_VK_VALIDATION = 'sync'
cmake --workflow --preset vs2026
ctest --preset vs2026 -L gpu        # only the GPU tests, after a build
```

Linux, including WSL (whose GPU driver reports below Vulkan 1.4, so the tests use lavapipe):

```sh
cmake -P ../rawreel-workspace/tools/check-vulkan-device.cmake    # needs a Vulkan 1.4 device
export DVV_REQUIRE_GPU=1 DVV_VK_VALIDATION=sync DVV_VK_VALIDATION_REQUIRED=1
cmake --workflow --preset ninja-clang
```

`dvv-gpu-info` (`_build/dvv-gpu/<preset>/<config>/`) lists the Vulkan devices, their versions,
missing requirements and optional features (`--json` for JSON); it exits with 0 when a usable device
exists and 4 otherwise.

## Checks
````

- [ ] **Step 3: Check the Markdown and the privacy lint**

The check renders each file with markdown-it-py (CommonMark with tables) and fails on an odd number of fence lines or a line starting with `|` that did not become a table row (the §11 table sits inside a list item and must render there):

````powershell
python -m venv build-venv; ./build-venv/Scripts/python -m pip install --quiet --disable-pip-version-check markdown-it-py==4.2.0
@'
import sys
from markdown_it import MarkdownIt
md = MarkdownIt("commonmark").enable("table")
bad = False
for path in sys.argv[1:]:
    text = open(path, encoding="utf-8").read()
    tokens = md.parse(text)
    covered = set()
    for t in tokens:
        if t.type in ("table_open", "fence") and t.map:
            covered.update(range(*t.map))
    lines = text.splitlines()
    stray = [i + 1 for i, line in enumerate(lines) if line.lstrip().startswith("|") and i not in covered]
    ticks = sum(1 for line in lines if line.lstrip().startswith("```"))
    tables = sum(1 for t in tokens if t.type == "table_open")
    fences = sum(1 for t in tokens if t.type == "fence")
    print(f"{path}: tables={tables} fences={fences} stray={stray}")
    bad = bad or bool(ticks % 2 or stray)
print("MARKDOWN-FAILED" if bad else "MARKDOWN-OK")
'@ | Set-Content -Encoding utf8 build-venv/mdcheck.py
./build-venv/Scripts/python build-venv/mdcheck.py rawreel-workspace/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-19-rawreel-design.md rawreel-workspace/README.md
cmake -DREPO=rawreel-workspace -DMODE=tree "-DEXTRA_TERMS=$env:USERNAME;$env:COMPUTERNAME" -P rawreel-workspace/tools/privacy-lint.cmake
````

with

````markdown
```

## Writing a component

A component implements an interface (a class with a virtual destructor) and registers itself:

```cpp
// greeting.cpp, in the component's own static library
class Greeting final : public IGreeting, public dvv::Managed<Greeting> {
public:
    Greeting(dvv::CreationKey<dvv::ComponentRegistry> key,
             [[maybe_unused]] const dvv::ParameterValues& values)
        : Managed(std::move(key)) { /* read values */ }

    static dvv::ComponentInfo info() noexcept {
        return {.id = "example.greeting", .name = "Greeting", .kind = dvv::Kind::Tool};
    }
    static const dvv::ParameterSchema& schema() {
        static const dvv::ParameterSchema SCHEMA =
            dvv::ParameterSchema::Builder()
                .addInt("repeat", "Repeat", {.min = 1, .max = 10, .defaultValue = 1})
                .build()
                .value();
        return SCHEMA;
    }
    static std::expected<void, dvv::Unavailable> probe(const dvv::Capabilities& caps) {
        return dvv::require(caps, {"gpu.compute"});
    }
    // IGreeting's functions ...
};
DVV_REGISTER(IGreeting, Greeting);
```

```cmake
add_library(greeting STATIC greeting.cpp)
target_link_libraries(greeting PUBLIC dvv::registry)
dvv_link_components(my_app greeting)     # never a plain target_link_libraries for components
```

```cpp
auto registry = dvv::ComponentRegistry::create(capabilities);   // in main, after static init
auto greeting = registry->create<IGreeting>("example.greeting", {{"repeat", std::int64_t{3}}});
```

Rules (`DVV_REGISTER` checks the first two at compile time, except the id):

- The class is `final`, derives `dvv::Managed<T>` (so it is neither copyable nor movable) and the
  interface, and its constructor takes `dvv::CreationKey<dvv::ComponentRegistry>` and the parameter
  values: only the registry can create it.
- `info()` is `noexcept` and returns a unique, stable id; `schema()` and `probe()` are static.
- `DVV_REGISTER(Interface, Type);` stands at namespace scope in a `.cpp` of a static component
  library, which is linked with `dvv_link_components()` (as a whole archive; otherwise the linker drops
  the unreferenced registration). A library linked both ways is rejected by CMake.
- Create the `ComponentRegistry` from `main`, never during static initialisation: registration happens
  before `main`. Component libraries are never loaded at run time.
- The registry runs `schema()` and `probe()` once. If one of them throws, or `probe()` fails, the
  component is listed as unavailable with the reason. A constructor that throws makes `create()` return
  `Errc::Internal` and disables the component for that registry; `std::bad_alloc` returns
  `Errc::OutOfMemory` and leaves it available. Two components with the same id are both unavailable.
- `create()` validates the values against the schema and fills in the defaults first; bad values are
  `Errc::Format`, an unknown or unavailable id `Errc::Unsupported`.

Licence: MIT.
````

In `dvv-gpu/README.md` (under "## Shaders") replace

```markdown

with

````markdown
installed with the target.

The Slang version is pinned twice: `DVV_SLANGC_VERSION` in `cmake/DvvShaders.cmake` and the Slang
archives in rawreel-workspace's `deps/versions.cmake`. Update both together.

## Compute pipelines and the pipeline cache

`ctx->createComputePipeline(my_shaders::addOne(), sizeof(AddOne))` makes a pipeline with one
push-constant range and no descriptor sets: shaders reach buffers through their device addresses,
passed in the push constants. The C++ push-constant struct (`AddOne` here) is written by hand; its
size must be a multiple of 4 and cover the shader's push-constant block. In a record function,
`recorder.dispatch(pipeline, std::as_bytes(std::span(&push, 1)), groups)` takes exactly that many
bytes.

The command recorder puts a global memory barrier before every command it records, because
synchronization validation cannot see device-address accesses (`tests/gpu_test_compute.cpp` fails
without it). Commands recorded through `raw()` get that barrier before them but must order
themselves among each other, and bring their own barriers for stages other than compute and
transfer.

Host-visible buffers are not flushed or invalidated automatically: call `flush()` after writing a
mapped buffer and before the GPU reads it, and `invalidate()` after the GPU work completed and
before reading it (both do nothing on host-coherent memory).

`ResourcePool::acquireBuffer()` returns a lease; a released lease returns the buffer to the pool,
which hands it out again only after the GPU has finished every submission made or being recorded at
its return. Keep the lease alive until the submission that uses the buffer has completed.

`ContextOptions::pipelineCacheFile` names the pipeline-cache file (empty: in memory only);
`defaultPipelineCacheFile(app, device)` gives one file per device in the user's cache directory. The
file is checked before use (header, device, a dvv checksum); a damaged or foreign file is ignored
with a log line. The cache is saved atomically by `savePipelineCache()` and, if it changed, when
the context closes.

## GPU tests and dvv-gpu-info

The GPU tests are a separate executable (`dvv_gpu.gpu`, plus `dvv_gpu.gpu_info` for the tool; CTest
label `gpu`). Without a usable Vulkan 1.4 device they exit with 77, which CTest reports as skipped.
CTest points the loader at the validation layer of the third-party prefix (`VK_ADD_LAYER_PATH`), and
every test fails if a validation message was counted. The compute demo is
`tests/gpu_test_compute.cpp`. Environment:

- `DVV_REQUIRE_GPU=1`: a missing device fails the tests instead of skipping them (Linux CI).
- `DVV_VK_VALIDATION=off|on|sync`: validation mode; default `on` in Debug when the layer is present.
- `DVV_VK_VALIDATION_REQUIRED=1`: a missing validation layer is an error.

```sh
DVV_REQUIRE_GPU=1 DVV_VK_VALIDATION=sync DVV_VK_VALIDATION_REQUIRED=1 ctest --preset ninja-clang -L gpu
```

ThreadSanitizer builds give the GPU tests `tests/tsan.supp`, which suppresses only reports with a
frame inside the (uninstrumented) Vulkan driver; do not set `TSAN_OPTIONS` yourself for them.

`dvv-gpu-info [--json]` prints the loader version, the validation mode and every device: name, type,
Vulkan and driver versions, device-local memory, missing requirements and optional features
(`shaderInt16`, `shaderFloat16`, `hostImageCopy`, `VK_EXT_memory_budget`); the device that would be
selected is marked. Exit code 0 when a usable device exists, 4 when there is none or no loader, 1 for
a bad argument. To use the prefix's validation layer outside CTest, set `VK_ADD_LAYER_PATH` to
`<prefix>/bin` (Windows) or `<prefix>/share/vulkan/explicit_layer.d` (Linux).

## Third-party code

- Vulkan-Hpp is configured in one private header, `src/vk.h` (dynamic dispatch, no exceptions, no
  prototypes); every call passes its instance or device dispatcher. Public headers use the Vulkan C
  types only.
- `VkBootstrap.cpp` is compiled into `dvv_gpu` from the prefix (`src/vk-bootstrap/`), so it always
  uses the same runtime library as the rest. Like all third-party sources compiled into a dvv target,
  it never fails the build on warnings: its source options cancel warnings as errors (`/WX-`,
  `-Wno-error`), and its warnings stay visible. VMA's implementation has its own translation unit
  (`src/vma.cpp`).
- Third-party include directories are always `SYSTEM`. The clang-tidy header filter
  (`.*/(include/dvv|src)/.*`) covers the private headers in `src/` but also matches any third-party
  path containing `/src/`; `SYSTEM` keeps such headers out of the warnings and the checks.

Licence: MIT.
````

## Changes during execution

- Tasks 12 and 13: the task reviews and two user decisions led to two commits after Task 13 in dvv-gpu.
  The code blocks of Tasks 12 and 13 above already show the final code.
  - `fix: reset the validation count after falling back without the layer`: when the validation
    layer's library fails to load, the loader reports it at ERROR through the instance-creation
    messenger, so the count is reset before the second attempt. The Windows loader (1.4.341)
    returns `VK_ERROR_OUT_OF_HOST_MEMORY` for this case and the Linux loader (1.3.275)
    `VK_ERROR_LAYER_NOT_PRESENT`; both lead to the retry when validation is not required.
  - `build: compile the vk-bootstrap source without warnings as errors` (user decision): third-party
    sources compiled into a dvv target keep their warnings visible, but the warnings never fail the
    build (`/WX-` on MSVC, `-Wno-error` on Clang, as source-file options).
- Task 17: under ThreadSanitizer, the multi-threaded retire test failed in about 4 of 10 runs on
  lavapipe. The reports came from inside the driver: it uses futex locks that TSan cannot see, and
  no dvv-gpu frame was involved. The commit therefore adds `tests/tsan.supp`
  (`race:libvulkan_lvp.so`) and sets `TSAN_OPTIONS=suppressions=...` for the GPU tests when
  `DVV_SANITIZE` is `thread`.
- Task 21: the task review found that the "too many groups" refusal test used `UINT32_MAX`, but
  Vulkan allows `maxComputeWorkGroupCount[0]` up to `UINT32_MAX` (AMD drivers report that value). A
  commit after Task 22, `test: take the refused group count from the device limits`, changes the
  test to dispatch limit + 1 on an axis whose limit is below `UINT32_MAX`. If no axis qualifies, the
  test skips that case with a message.
- Final whole-branch review: three reviewers covered dvv-gpu, dvv-base with dvv-registry, and
  rawreel-workspace. They found no Critical problems. One fix wave per repository then applied
  their "fix now" lists:
  - dvv-base and dvv-registry (`test: match Clang's private-constructor diagnostic ...`,
    `fix: apply the final-review fixes`): `create<I>` records the caller's creation site, enum
    options must not start or end with white space, the parameter-type `static_assert`s, a
    concurrent `ComponentRegistry` test, and include and final-newline hygiene.
  - dvv-gpu (five commits):
    - the instance is retried without validation only when `vkCreateInstance` itself failed;
    - `~ResourcePool` no longer calls `trim()`;
    - `toJson` moved to `dvv/gpu/gpu_info_json.h`, and `dvv-gpu-info --help` lists its exit codes;
    - a test runs 64 submissions in flight under TSan;
    - documentation wording fixes.
  - rawreel-workspace: `bootstrap --force`, or a deleted prefix, now really rebuilds, because the
    ExternalProject stamps are removed. The device check prints vulkaninfo's warnings, and the
    spec and README match the code.
  - Deferred with a reason: a UTF-8 environment API in dvv-base (for non-ANSI paths in
    `VK_LAYER_PATH` on Windows), to M1b. Renaming `ComponentRegistry::create<I>` to
    `createComponent<I>` waits for a user decision.
- User decision: dvv-gpu's clang-tidy `HeaderFilterRegex` stays `'.*/(include/dvv|src)/.*'`.
  Third-party headers must always be included as SYSTEM, which Task 24 documents.
